Classifying Fano 4 4 -folds with a rational fibration onto a 3 3 -fold
C. Casagrande, S.A. Secci

Abstract
. We study smooth, complex Fano 4 4 -folds X X with a rational contraction onto a 3 3 -fold, namely a rational map X ⇢ Y X\dasharrow Y that factors as a sequence of flips X ⇢ X ~ X\dasharrow\widetilde{X} followed by a surjective morphism X ~ → Y \widetilde{X}\to Y with connected fibers, where Y Y is normal, projective, and dim Y = 3 \dim Y=3 . We show that if X X has a rational contraction onto a 3 3 -fold and X X is not a product of del Pezzo surfaces, then the Picard number ρ X \rho_{X} of X X is at most 9 9 ; this bound is sharp. As an application, we show that every Fano 4 4 -fold X X with ρ X = 12 \rho_{X}=12 is isomorphic to a product of surfaces, thus improving the result by the first named author that shows the same for ρ X > 12 \rho_{X}>12 . We also give a classification result for Fano 4 4 -folds X X , not products of surfaces, having a “special” rational contraction X ⇢ Y X\dasharrow Y with dim Y = 3 \dim Y=3 , ρ X − ρ Y = 2 \rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=2 , and ρ X ≥ 7 \rho_{X}\geq 7 ; we show that there are only three possible families. Then we prove that the first family exists if ρ X = 7 \rho_{X}=7 , and that the second family exists if and only if ρ X = 7 \rho_{X}=7 . This provides the first examples of Fano 4 4 -folds with ρ X ≥ 7 \rho_{X}\geq 7 different from products of del Pezzo surfaces and from the Fano models of Bl pts ⁡ ℙ 4 \operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4} . We also construct three new families with ρ X = 6 \rho_{X}=6 . Finally we show that if a Fano 4 4 -fold X X has Lefschetz defect δ X = 2 \delta_{X}=2 , then ρ X ≤ 6 \rho_{X}\leq 6 ; this bound is again sharp.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
14J45,14J35,14E30

1.
Introduction

Let
X
be a smooth, complex Fano
4
-fold, and
\rho_{X}
its Picard number. Recall that since
X
is Fano,
\rho_{X}
coincides with the second Betti number of
X
, therefore it is a topological invariant and is constant in smooth families. It is well known that there are finitely many families of Fano
4
-folds, and it is a recent result that when the Picard number is large,
X
must be a product of surfaces:

Theorem 1.1

(
[
Cas24
]
, Th. 1.1)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold. If
\rho_{X}>12
, then
X\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
, where
S_{i}
are del Pezzo surfaces.

Let us point out that all known examples of Fano
4
-folds that are not products have
\rho\leq 9
, hence it is not known whether for
\rho=10,11,12
there are only products of surfaces. For the case
\rho=12
, there is the following partial result.

Theorem 1.2

(
[
Cas24
]
, Th. 4.3)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold. If
\rho_{X}=12
and
X
is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, then there is a rational contraction
X\dasharrow Y
with
\dim Y=3
.

A
rational contraction
is a rational map
X\dasharrow Y
that factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}Y
where
\xi
is a sequence of flips,
f
is surjective with connected fibers, and
Y
is normal and projective (namely
f
is a contraction).

In this paper we study Fano
4
-folds
X
having a rational contraction
X\dasharrow Y
with
\dim Y=3
, and such that
X
is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, as above.
We have different motivations and goals: first, to describe the geometry of
X
, especially when
\rho_{X}
is large, and possibly to give classification results; then to determine a sharp bound on
\rho_{X}
, and to use it to show that Fano
4
-folds with Picard number 12 are products of surfaces; to construct new families of Fano
4
-folds with large Picard number (at least 6); and finally to give a sharp bound on the Picard number of Fano
4
-folds with Lefschetz defect
2
(see below).
As an outcome we get results in different directions, let us explain them separately.

Bounding the Picard number.
Our first goal is to bound the Picard number of Fano
4
-folds with a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, and our result is the following.

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold that is not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and having a rational contraction

X\dasharrow Y
with
\dim Y=3
. Then
\rho_{X}\leq 9
.

This improves the previous bound
\rho\leq 12
obtained in
[
Cas20
, Th. 1.2]
, and it is sharp,
as the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{8\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho=9
and a rational contraction onto
\operatorname{Bl}_{7\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
, see Ex.
7.2
.

Fano
4
-folds with
\rho=12
.

As a straightforward consequence of Th.
1.2
and
1.3
we improve the bound in Th.
1.1
.

Corollary 1.4
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}=12
. Then
X\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
where
S_{i}
are del Pezzo surfaces.

Fano
4
-folds with Lefschetz defect
2
.

The Lefschetz defect of a Fano variety
X
is an invariant defined as follows. Let
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
be the real vector space of one-cycles in
X
, with real coefficients, up to numerical equivalence. For any prime divisor
\iota\colon D\hookrightarrow X
, the push-forward gives a linear map
\iota_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(D)\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
, and we consider the image

\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X):=\iota_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(D))\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X),

so that
\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X)
is the linear subspace of
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
spanned by classes of curves in
D
. The
Lefschetz defect
of
X
is defined as

(1.5)

\delta_{X}:=\max\{\operatorname{codim}\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X)\,|\,D\text{ a prime divisor in }X\}.

We refer the reader to
[
Cas23
]
for a survey on
\delta_{X}
, and recall the
following result.

Theorem 1.6

(
[
Cas12
]
, Th. 1.1)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano variety. If
\delta_{X}\geq 4
, then
X\cong S\times Z
, where
S
is a del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{S}=\delta_{X}+1
.

Therefore when
X
is not a product, we have
\delta_{X}\leq 3
.
Fano varieties with
\delta_{X}=3
do not need to be products, but for them
a structure theorem is given in
[
CRS22
]
, which in dimension
4
yields the following.

Theorem 1.7

(
[
CRS22
]
, Prop. 1.5)
.

Smooth Fano
4
-folds with
\delta_{X}=3
are classified.
Either
X\cong S_{1}\times S_{2}
where
S_{i}
are del Pezzo surfaces, or
\rho_{X}\in\{5,6\}
. There are 6 families for
\rho_{X}=5
, and 11 for
\rho_{X}=6
.

We believe that, to study Fano varieties with large
\rho_{X}
, it is important to investigate the next case
\delta_{X}=2
, by looking for a special geometrical structure, as started in
[
Cas14
]
.
In dimension
4
, as an application of our results, we prove the following.

Theorem 1.8
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\delta_{X}=2
. Then
\rho_{X}\leq 6
.

We note that this bound is sharp too, as it is achieved by
(\operatorname{Bl}_{2\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2})^{2}
(see Rem.
2.1
). However we are not aware of other examples with
\rho=6
, while we provide several (known and new) examples of Fano
4
-folds
X
with
\delta_{X}=2
and
\rho_{X}\leq 5
, see §
7.4
and §
7.6
.

New families of Fano
4
-folds with
\rho=7
.

We already recalled that all known examples of Fano
4
-folds that are not products of surfaces have
\rho\leq 9
. More precisely,
for
\rho=7,8,9
there is only one known family, given by the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{r\,\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
for
r=6,7,8
, see Ex.
7.2
. We point out that also for
\rho=6
there are very few known families (again excluding products):
6
toric
[
Bat99
]
,
2
non toric with
\delta_{X}=3

[
CRS22
, Prop. 7.1]
, and one in
[
Man24
]
.

We construct two new families with
\rho=7
, which are the first examples of Fano
4
-folds with
\rho\geq 7
different from the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
and products of del Pezzo surfaces, as follows (see §
7.2
and §
7.3
).

Proposition 1.9
.

Let
r\in\{0,\dotsc,4\}
and let
W
be the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
(see Ex.
7.2
), with
q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}\in\mathbb{P}^{4}
general points.
Let
A\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
be one of the following:

(i)

a general cubic rational normal scroll containing

q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}
;

(ii)

a general sextic (singular) K3 surface with
\operatorname{Sing}(A)=\{q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}\}
,
having rational double points of type
A_{1}
or
A_{2}
in
q_{i}
for every
i
, and contained in a smooth quadric hypersurface.

Let
S\subset W
be the transform of
A
, and
X\to W
the blow-up of
S
.
Then
X
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold
with
\rho_{X}=r+3\in\{3,\dotsc,7\}
.

We note that this gives also two new families with
\rho=6
, and besides these we construct an additional new family with
\rho=6
(§
7.5
). These new families all have a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, and their
construction has been suggested by
our study of Fano
4
-folds with such a rational contraction, as we explain below.

There are a few cases in our constructions that we leave open, and could lead to more examples, see Questions
7.1
,
7.6
, and
7.25
.

Special rational contractions.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction with
\dim Y=3
. We say that
f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}
is special if
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and, when we factor
f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}
as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}Y
where
\xi
is a sequence of flips and
f
a contraction, we have that
f
has at most isolated
2
-dimensional fibers (and no
3
-dimensional fibers). Special rational contractions have especially good properties, see
[
Cas20
, §3 and §6]
.

It follows from
[
Cas20
]
that, when
X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, it also has a
special
rational contraction onto a (possibly different)
3
-fold. In our setting we show the following.

Theorem 1.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and having a rational contraction onto a
3
-dimensional variety.

Then there exists a special rational contraction
X\dasharrow Y
, where
Y
is a weak Fano
1
1

1

Y
is weak Fano if
-K_{Y}
is nef and big.

3
-fold with at most isolated, locally factorial, and canonical singularities, and
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}\in\{1,2\}
.

Therefore to study the geometry of
X
as in Th.
1.10
above we are reduced to study separately the two cases where

X
has an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold, and where
there is a special rational contraction
X\dasharrow Y
of relative Picard number two, with
\dim Y=3
.

Classification results.

In the case of relative Picard number two, in the range
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, we show that there are only three possibilities for
X
.

Theorem 1.11
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and having a special rational contraction

X\dasharrow Y
with
\dim Y=3
and
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=2
.
Then
\rho_{X}\in\{7,8,9\}
and

X
is the blow-up of
W
along a normal surface
S
,
where
W
is the Fano model of
\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
(see Ex.
7.2
), and
S\subset W
is the transform of a surface
A\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
containing the blown-up points, as in one of the following cases:

(i)

A
is a cubic scroll;

(ii)

A
is a sextic K3 surface, with rational double points of type
A_{1}
or
A_{2}
at the blown-up points, and
\rho_{X}=7
;

(iii)

A
is a cone over a twisted cubic.

In cases
(i)
and
(ii)
the surface
S
is smooth, while in
(iii)

S
has one singular point, given by the vertex of the cone.
Moreover
Y
is smooth, and up to flops
Y\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
.

With Prop.
1.9

we show that cases
(i)
and
(ii)
do occur for
\rho_{X}\leq 7
, while we do not explore case
(iii)
.

Techniques and strategy of proof.
Let
X
be a Fano
4
-fold and
f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction with
\dim Y=3
. First of all, by the results in
[
Cas20
]
, we can assume that
f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}
is special (see Prop.
3.11
).

Recall that, when
\delta_{X}\geq 3
, either
X
is a product of surfaces, or
\rho_{X}\in\{5,6\}
and the possible
X
are classified, by Th.
1.6
and
1.7
; therefore we can also suppose that

\delta_{X}\leq 2
.

Let us consider a factorization of
f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}
as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}Y
; we can assume that
f
is
K
-negative. Then
Y
has at most isolated, locally factorial, and canonical singularities, and outside the (finitely many)
2
-dimensional fibers,
f
is a conic bundle (see
3.3
). By studying the connected components of the discriminant divisor of
f
in
Y
,
we show that when
f
is special and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
,
then either
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}\leq 2
, or
\delta_{X}=2
(Lemma
3.12
).

We first treat the case where
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}\leq 2
.
We assume that
\rho_{Y}\geq 5
and follow the same approach as in
[
Cas13
]
and
[
Cas20
]
, that led to the bound
\rho_{X}\leq 12
. We show that
Y
is weak Fano, and that (up to flops) there is a blow-up
Y\to Y_{0}
of
r
distinct smooth points, where
Y_{0}
is weak Fano with
\rho_{Y_{0}}\leq 2
(
5.12
, Lemma
5.16
, proof of Th.
6.3
).

In order to bound
\rho_{X}
, we need to bound the number
r
of blown-up points, and for this we use the anticanonical degree. Since
0<-K_{Y}^{3}=-K_{Y_{0}}^{3}-8r
, we give bounds on
-K_{Y_{0}}^{3}
in our setting, using several results from the literature on singular Fano or weak Fano
3
-folds, see Section
4
and references therein.

In the elementary case (namely
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=1
), this is enough to show that
\rho_{X}\leq 9
; if moreover
Y
is smooth, then we show that (up to flops) there are only six possibilities
for
Y_{0}
and
Y
(Cor.
6.4
).

The case where
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=2
is studied in much more detail, as besides bounding the Picard number, we classify
X
as stated in Th.
1.11
. This
is done in Section
5
which is the heart of the paper, and
requires a long and articulated analysis that allows to prove first that
Y\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and then to identify
X
. We refer the reader to
5.2

for an overview of the proof.

Finally let us consider
Fano
4
-folds
X
with Lefschetz defect
\delta_{X}=2
. If
X
is a product of surfaces, it is easy to see that
\rho_{X}\leq 6
. Otherwise, by
[
Cas14
]
either
X
has a special rational contraction
X\dasharrow Y
where
\dim Y=3
and
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=2
, or it has a flat contraction
X\to S
onto a surface. In the second case we get easily
\rho_{X}\leq 5

by applying results from
[
Cas08
]
. In the first case we apply our previous results on the case of relative Picard number two; more precisely
we exclude that
\rho_{X}\geq 7
thanks to our classification (Thm.
1.11
).

Outline of the paper.

In Section
2
we set up the notation and give some preliminary results. In particular in §
2.2
we recall the properties of fixed prime divisors in Fano
4
-folds with
\rho\geq 7
, that will be crucial in the sequel.

Section
3
is about special rational contractions from smooth
4
-folds to
3
-folds. First we consider
K
-negative special contractions
f\colon X\to Y
where
X
is a smooth projective
4
-fold and
\dim Y=3
; then we introduce special rational contractions of Fano
4
-folds onto
3
-folds, and their properties.

Section
4
is an auxiliary section, where we present some results on Fano and weak Fano
3
-folds
Y
with locally factorial and canonical singularities, in particular with respect to the
anticanonical degree
-K_{Y}^{3}
; these will be needed in the rest of the paper.

In Section
5
we treat the case of relative Picard number
2
, and prove Th.
1.11
.

Then in Section
6
we treat the elementary case and the case of Lefschetz defect
2
, proving Theorems
1.3
,
1.8
, and
1.10
.

Finally Section
7
is devoted to the construction of new families and examples, we prove Prop.
1.9
and ask some open questions on possible further new examples.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Notations

We work over the field of complex numbers.
We refer the reader to
[
KM98
]
for the terminology and standard results in birational geometry, and to
[
HK00
]
for Mori dream spaces.

Let
X
be a normal and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial quasi-projective variety.
A
contraction
is a surjective, projective map
f\colon X\to Y
, with connected fibers, where
Y
is normal and quasi-projective.
We denote by
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X/Y)
the vector space of one cycles in
X
, with coefficients in
\mathbb{R}
, contracted to points by
f
, up to numerical equivalence.
We say that
f
is
elementary
if
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(X/Y)=1
, and
f
is
K
-negative
is
-K_{X}
is
f
-ample.

Let
X
be projective. As usual we denote by
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
(respectively
\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
)
the vector space of
\mathbb{R}
-divisors in
X
(respectively one cycles in
X
with coefficients in
\mathbb{R}
) up to numerical equivalence. We denote by
[D]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
the class of a divisor, and
\equiv
stands for numerical equivalence.

An elementary contraction
f
is
of type
(a,b)
if
\dim\operatorname{Exc}(f)=a
and
\dim f(\operatorname{Exc}(f))=b
.
Additionally, when
\dim X=4
, we say that
f
is of type

(3,b)^{\text{\it sm}}
, with
b\in\{0,1,2\}
, if
Y
is smooth and
f
is the blow-up of a smooth, irreducible subvariety of dimension
b
. Still when
\dim X=4
, we say that
f
is of type
(3,0)^{Q}
if
\operatorname{Exc}(f)\cong Q
where
Q
is a
3
-dimensional quadric,
f(Q)=\{pt\}
, and
\mathcal{N}_{\operatorname{Exc}(f)/Q}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Q}(-1)
; then
Q
is either smooth or the cone over a smooth quadric surface, see
[
Cas17
, Lemma 2.19]
.

We denote by
\operatorname{NE}(f)
the face of
\operatorname{NE}(X)
generated by classes of curves contracted to points by
f
.

Suppose that
X
is a Mori dream space. An
extremal ray
R
is one dimensional face of
\operatorname{NE}(X)
.
Let
f\colon X\to Y
be the associated elementary contraction (namely
R=\operatorname{NE}(f)
); we set
\operatorname{Locus}(R):=\operatorname{Exc}(f)
.
For a divisor
D
on
X
we write

D\cdot R>0,=0,<0
if
D\cdot\gamma>0,=0,<0
for
\gamma\in R
non-zero. We also say that
R
or
f
are
D
-positive,
D
-trivial,
D
-negative respectively.

By a flip we mean the flip of a small extremal ray
R
, or equivalently of a small elementary contraction, in the sense of
[
KM98
, Def. 3.33]
. We say that the flip is

D
-negative if
R
is. In dimension
3
, as customary a flop is the flip of a
K
-trivial extremal ray.

We set
D^{\perp}:=\{\gamma\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)|D\cdot\gamma=0\}
.
In a real vector space
N
of finite dimension, we denote by

\langle\gamma_{1},\dotsc,\gamma_{r}\rangle
the convex cone spanned by
\gamma_{1},\dotsc,\gamma_{r}
.

In
\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
we denote by
\operatorname{Mov}(X)
(respectively
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
) the convex cone spanned by classes of movable (respectively effective) divisors. Since
X
is a Mori dream space, both cones are polyhedral.

A
fixed prime divisor
is a prime divisor
D
such that
\operatorname{Bs}|mD|=D
for every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

A
small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification (SQM)
of
X
is a birational map
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
that factors as a finite sequence of flips.
A
rational contraction
is a rational map
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
that factors as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\dasharrow}}X^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\to}}Y
, where
\varphi
is a SQM and
f^{\prime}
is a contraction;
f
is
elementary
if
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=1
.
An elementary rational contraction can be divisorial, small, or of fiber type, depending on the corresponding property of
f^{\prime}
.

Let
X
be a normal and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial projective variety. We say that
X
is
log Fano
if there exists an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\Delta
such that
(X,\Delta)
has klt singularities and
-(K_{X}+\Delta)
is ample; in particular
-K_{X}
is big. If
X
is log Fano, then
X
is a Mori dream space, by
[
BCHM10
, Cor. 1.3.2]
; moreover if
X\dasharrow Y
is a rational contraction with
Y

\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, then
Y
is still log Fano, by
[
PS09
, Lemma 2.8]
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective
4
-fold. An
exceptional plane
is a surface
L\subset X
such that
L\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
\mathcal{N}_{L/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(-1)^{\oplus 2}
; we denote by
C_{L}\subset L
a line in
L
, note that
-K_{X}\cdot C_{L}=1
. An
exceptional line
is a curve
\ell\subset X
such that
\ell\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\ell/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-1)^{\oplus 3}
; note that
K_{X}\cdot\ell=1
.

A
node
is an ordinary double point.

We denote by
\mathbb{P}^{2}\bullet\mathbb{P}^{2}
the union of two planes in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
intersecting in one point.

If
\iota\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
is a closed subset, we set
\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z,X):=\iota_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z))\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.

We give a few preliminary results that are needed in the sequel; recall the definition of Lefschetz defect (
1.5
).

Remark 2.1

(
[
Cas23
]
, Lemma 5)
.

Let
X=Y\times Z
where
Y
and
Z
are smooth Fano varieties; then
\delta_{X}=\max\{\delta_{Y},\delta_{Z}\}
. Moreover it follows from the definition of Lefschetz defect that a del Pezzo surface
S
has
\delta_{S}=\rho_{S}-1
.

Remark 2.2
.

Let
X
be a normal and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial projective variety,
Z\subset X
a closed subset, and
D\subset X
a prime divisor such that
Z\cap D=\emptyset
.
Then
\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z,X)\subseteq D^{\perp}\subset\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)
.
Indeed we have
D\cdot C=0
for every curve
C\subset Z
.

Remark 2.3
.

Let
X
be a normal and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial projective variety,
Z\subset X
a closed subset, and
f\colon X\to Y
a contraction.
Then
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z,X)\leq\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(f(Z),Y)+\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}
.

Indeed consider the pushforward
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(Y)
; we have
f_{*}(\mathcal{N}_{1}(Z,X))=\mathcal{N}_{1}(f(Z),Y)
and
\dim\ker f_{*}=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
\alpha\colon X\to W
be the blow-up of a smooth projective
4
-fold along a smooth irreducible surface
S
,
with exceptional divisor
E\subset X
.

Then
\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X})_{|E}
is ample on
E
if and only if the vector bundle

\mathcal{N}_{S/W}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{W}(-K_{W})_{|S}\cong\mathcal{N}_{S/W}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{S}(-K_{S})

is ample on
S
.
Moreover we have the following:

\displaystyle K_{X}^{4}=K_{W}^{4}-3(K_{W|S})^{2}-2K_{S}\cdot K_{W|S}+c_{2}(\mathcal{N}_{S/W})-K_{S}^{2},

\displaystyle K_{X}^{2}\cdot c_{2}(X)=K_{W}^{2}\cdot c_{2}(W)-12\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})+2K_{S}^{2}-2K_{S}\cdot K_{W|S}-2c_{2}(\mathcal{N}_{W/Y}),

\displaystyle\chi(X,-K_{X})=\chi(W,-K_{W})-\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})-\frac{1}{2}\bigl((K_{W|S})^{2}+K_{S}\cdot K_{W|S}\bigr).

Proof.

We have
E\cong\mathbb{P}_{S}(\mathcal{N}_{S/W}^{\vee})
with tautological class
\eta\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}(-E)_{|E}
. Moreover

\mathcal{O}_{E}(K_{E})=\alpha_{|E}^{*}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{S}(K_{S})\otimes\det\mathcal{N}_{S/W}^{\vee}\bigr)\otimes\eta^{\otimes(-2)},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathcal{O}_{W}(-K_{W})_{|S}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}(-K_{S})\otimes\det\mathcal{N}_{S/W},

and
K_{E}=(K_{X}+E)_{|E}
.
We get that

\mathcal{O}_{X}(-K_{X})_{|E}=\eta\otimes\alpha_{|E}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{W}(-K_{W})_{|S})
is the tautological class for
E=\mathbb{P}_{S}(\mathcal{N}_{S/W}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{W}(-K_{W})_{|S})
.

Finally we recall that for a vector two vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
we have
\mathcal{E}\cong\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes\det(\mathcal{E})
, and the formulae are from
[
CR22
, Lemma 3.2]
.
∎

Remark 2.5
.

Let
Y
be a smooth quasi-projective variety,
X:=\mathbb{P}_{Y}(\mathcal{E})
where
\mathcal{E}
is a rank two vector bundle on
Y
, and
\pi\colon X\to Y
the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle. Set
L:=\det\mathcal{E}\in\operatorname{Pic}(Y)
.

Let
C\subset Y
be a smooth projective rational curve and set
S:=\pi^{-1}(C)\cong\mathbb{F}_{e}
, where
\mathbb{F}_{e}
is the Hirzebruch surface and
e\geq 0
. Let
\Gamma^{-}
and
\Gamma^{+}
be respectively the negative section and a positive section of
\pi_{|S}\colon S\to C
(namely
(\Gamma^{-})^{2}=-e
and
(\Gamma^{+})^{2}=e
in
S
). Then
L\cdot C\equiv e\mod 2
and

-K_{X}\cdot\Gamma^{-}=-K_{Y}\cdot C-e,\hskip 10.00002pt\hskip 10.00002pt-K_{X}\cdot\Gamma^{+}=-K_{Y}\cdot C+e.

Proof.

We have

-K_{X}=\pi^{*}(-K_{Y})-K_{\pi}
and

-K_{X|S}=\pi^{*}(-K_{Y})_{|S}-K_{\pi|S}=\pi^{*}(-K_{Y})_{|S}-K_{S}+(\pi_{|S})^{*}K_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}.

In particular if
\Gamma
is
\Gamma^{+}
or
\Gamma^{-}

we get

-K_{X}\cdot\Gamma=-K_{Y}\cdot C-K_{S}\cdot\Gamma-2
, which yields the formulae above since

-K_{S}\cdot\Gamma^{-}=2-e
and
-K_{S}\cdot\Gamma^{+}=2+e
.

To see that
L\cdot C\equiv e\mod 2
, write
\mathcal{E}_{|C}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(a)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(b)
with
a\leq b
. Then
L\cdot C=a+b
and
e=b-a
.
∎

Finally recall some results on
K
-negative contractions.

Theorem 2.6

(
[
AW98
]
, Theorem on p. 256)
.

Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective
4
-fold and
f\colon X\to Y
a
K
-negative divisorial elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
. Then
f
can have at most finitely many
2
-dimensional fibers over
y_{1},\dotsc,y_{r}
,
\operatorname{Sing}(Y)\subseteq\{y_{1},\dotsc,y_{r}\}
, and
Y
is locally factorial and has at most nodes as singularities. Moreover over
Y\smallsetminus\{y_{1},\dotsc,y_{r}\}

f
is just the blow-up of a smooth, irreducible surface.

Theorem 2.7

(
[
Del14
]
, Th. 2.2)
.

Let
X
be a normal and locally factorial projective variety with canonical singularities, and with at most finitely many non-terminal points. Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a
K
-negative birational elementary contraction, with fibers of dimension
\leq 1
. Then
f
is divisorial.

Theorem 2.8

(
[
Kaw89
]
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth, quasi-projective
4
-fold, and
f\colon X\to Y
a
K
-negative small elementary contraction. Then
\operatorname{Exc}(f)
is a finite, disjoint union of exceptional planes.

Theorem 2.9

(
[
Kac98
]
, Th. 1.1, Cor. 2.2 and references therein)
.

Let
X
be a projective
4
-fold with at most locally factorial, terminal, isolated l.c.i. singularities. Let
f\colon X\to Y
a
K
-negative small elementary contraction. Then for every irreducible component
L
of
\operatorname{Exc}(f)
we have
(L,-K_{X|L})\cong(\mathbb{P}^{2},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1))
.

Proposition 2.10

(
[
Cas14
]
, Prop. 3.7(2))
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
a birational, rational contraction such that
X^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial. Set
P:=X^{\prime}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1})
. Then for every irreducible curve
C\subset X^{\prime}
with
-K_{X^{\prime}}\cdot C=1
we have either
C\subset P
or
C\cap P=\emptyset
.

A
conic bundle
is a projective morphism
f\colon X\to Y
where
X
is a quasi-projective variety with Gorenstein, log terminal singularities,
Y
is smooth, and there exists a rank
3
vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
on
Y
such that
X\subset\mathbb{P}_{Y}(\mathcal{E})
,
f
is the restriction of the projection
\pi\colon\mathbb{P}_{Y}(\mathcal{E})\to Y
, and every fiber of
f
is a plane conic in the corresponding fiber of
\pi
; see for instance
[
Bea77
, Ch. I]
.

Theorem 2.11

(
[
AW98
]
, Prop. 4.1 and references therein)
.

Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective variety and
f\colon X\to Y
a
K
-negative contraction such that every fiber of
f
has dimension one. Then
Y
is smooth and
f
is a conic bundle.

Proposition 2.12

(
[
Rom19b
]
, Prop. 1.3)
.

Let
X
be a Gorenstein quasi-projective variety with log terminal singularities and
f\colon X\to Y
a
K
-negative contraction with
Y
smooth, such that every fiber of
f
has dimension one. Then

f
is a conic bundle.

2.2.
Fano
4
-folds and fixed divisors

In this section we recall some results on the birational geometry of Fano
4
-folds. First of all, we describe in Lemma
2.13
the structure of SQM’s of a Fano
4
-fold. Then we recall a classification result for fixed prime divisors in Fano
4
-folds with Picard number
\geq 7
, there are only four possible types, and we describe the associated divisorial elementary rational contractions (Th.-Def.
2.16
). Finally we describe the possible relative positions of some pairs of fixed prime divisors that appear as exceptional divisors for the same birational map (Lemmas
2.17
-
2.19
).

Lemma 2.13

(
[
Cas13
]
, Rem. 3.6)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
a SQM.
We have the following:

(a)

\widetilde{X}
is smooth,
X\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi)=L_{1}\cup\cdots\cup L_{r}
where
L_{i}
are pairwise disjoint exceptional planes,
and

\widetilde{X}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1})=\ell_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\ell_{r}

where
\ell_{i}
are pairwise disjoint exceptional lines; moreover
\varphi
factors as

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}}

where
f
is the blow-up of
L_{1}\cup\cdots\cup L_{r}
and
g
is the blow-up of
\ell_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\ell_{r}
.

(b)

Let
C\subset\widetilde{X}
be an irreducible curve, different from
\ell_{1},\dotsc,\ell_{r}
, and intersecting
\ell_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\ell_{r}
in
s\geq 0
points; then
-K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot C\geq 1+s
.

(c)

If
-K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot C=1
, then
C\cap(\ell_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\ell_{r})=\emptyset
.

Lemma 2.14

(
[
Cas13
]
, Rem. 3.7)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
a rational contraction. Then there exists a SQM
\xi\colon X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
such that the composition
f\circ\xi^{-1}\colon\widetilde{X}\to Y
is regular and
K
-negative.

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 6
, and
D\subset X
the exceptional divisor of a divisorial elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
. Let
X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
be a SQM, and
\widetilde{D}\subset\widetilde{X}
the transform of
D
. Then
D
does not contain exceptional planes, and
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(D,X)=\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{D},\widetilde{X})
.

Proof.

By
[
Cas17
, Rem. 2.17(2)]

D
does not contain exceptional planes, thus the statement follows from
[
Cas13
, Cor. 3.14]
.
∎

Theorem - Definition 2.16

(
[
Cas17
]
, Th. 5.1, Cor. 5.2, Def. 5.3, Lemma 5.25, Def. 5.27)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, or
\rho_{X}=6
and
\delta_{X}\leq 2
, and
D
a fixed prime divisor in
X
.

(a)

There exists a unique diagram:

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y

where
\xi
is a SQM,
\sigma
is a divisorial elementary contraction with exceptional divisor the transform
\widetilde{D}
of
D
, and
Y
is Fano (possibly singular);

(b)

\sigma
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,0)^{Q}
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,2)
, and we define
D
to be
of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
(3,0)^{Q}
,
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, or
(3,2)
,
accordingly;

(c)

if
D
is of type
(3,2)
, then
X=\widetilde{X}
. In the other cases
\xi
factors as a sequence of
D
-negative and
K
-negative flips.

(d)

We define
C_{D}\subset D\subset X
to be the transform of a general irreducible curve
C_{\widetilde{D}}\subset\widetilde{D}\subset\widetilde{X}
contracted by
\sigma
, of minimal anticanonical degree. Then
C_{D}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
D\cdot C_{D}=-1
, and
C_{D}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\xi)
.

(e)

Given a SQM
X\dasharrow X^{\prime}
and a divisorial elementary contraction
\sigma^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
with
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma^{\prime})
the transform of
D
, there is a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}}

where
\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}
and
\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}
are SQM’s,
\widetilde{D}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle X})
,
and
\sigma(\widetilde{D})\subset\operatorname{dom}(\psi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y})
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold,
\varphi\colon X\dasharrow\widehat{X}
a SQM, and
E\subset\widehat{X}
a fixed prime divisor. We define
the type of
E
to be the type
of its transform
E_{\scriptscriptstyle X}\subset X
, and we define
C_{E}\subset E\subset\widehat{X}
to be the transform of
C_{E_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\subset X
. Note that, since
\dim(\widehat{X}\smallsetminus\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1}))=1
(see Lemma
2.13
(a)
), we have
C_{E}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\varphi^{-1})
.

We say that two fixed prime divisors
D,E
are
adjacent
if
[D],[E]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
generate a two dimensional face of
\operatorname{Eff}(X)
, and moreover

\langle[D],[E]\rangle\cap\operatorname{Mov}(X)=\{0\}
.

Lemma 2.17
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and let
D,E
be adjacent fixed prime divisors,
E
of type
(3,2)
, and
D
of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
or
(3,0)^{Q}
, such that
D\cap E\neq\emptyset
. Then
D\cdot C_{E}=0
and one of the following holds:

(i)

D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
E\cdot C_{D}=1
, and
E\cap L=\emptyset
for every exceptional plane
L\subset D
;

(ii)

D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
,
E\cdot C_{D}=0
, there exists an exceptional plane
L_{0}\subset D
such that
D\cdot C_{L_{0}}=-1
,
E\cdot C_{L_{0}}=1
,
C_{D}\equiv C_{E}+C_{L_{0}}
, and

E\cap L=\emptyset
for every exceptional plane
L\subset D
with
C_{L}\not\equiv C_{L_{0}}
;

(iii)

D
is of type
(3,0)^{Q}
,
E\cdot C_{D}=1
, there exists an exceptional plane
L_{0}\subset D
such that
D\cdot C_{L_{0}}=-1
,
E\cdot C_{L_{0}}=2
,
C_{D}\equiv C_{E}+C_{L_{0}}
, and

E\cap L=\emptyset
for every exceptional plane
L\subset D
with
C_{L}\not\equiv C_{L_{0}}
.

Proof.

Recall that
E
does not contain exceptional planes (Lemma
2.15
); then

D\cdot C_{E}=0
by
[
Cas20
, Lemma 4.9]
.
If
D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
and
E\cdot C_{D}>0
, then we have
(i)
by
[
Cas22
, Lemma 4.23]
.

Suppose that, if
D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, we have
E\cdot C_{D}=0
. Then we apply
[
Cas22
, Lemma 6.9, Prop. 6.1, Prop. 6.4, and Cor. 6.10]
, and get
(ii)
or
(iii)
.
∎

Given two adjacent fixed prime divisors
D
and
E
in
X
, up to a SQM we can contract both of them with divisorial elementary contractions. However if
D
and
E
intersect, in general the SQM and the type of divisorial elementary contractions may depend on the order with which we contract the two divisors. In the next lemmas we describe this situation for the cases given by Lemma
2.17
; this will be used in Section
5
. The vertical arrows (labeled by
\alpha
) are divisorial elementary contractions with exceptional divisor
E
or its transforms, while the horizontal arrows (labeled by
\sigma
) are divisorial elementary contractions with exceptional divisor
D
or its transforms.

Lemma 2.18

(
[
Cas22
]
, Lemma 4.23 and its proof)
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and let
D,E
be adjacent fixed prime divisors as in Lemma
2.17
(i)
.
Then we have a diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{{X_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{0}}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle W}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}}
\textstyle{{W_{0}}}

where:

\bullet

\alpha
,
\tilde{\alpha}
, and
\alpha_{0}
are divisorial elementary contractions of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
E
or its transforms;

\bullet

\xi
and
\sigma
are as in Th.-Def.
2.16

(a)
for
D
,
\sigma
blows-up a fiber
C\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
of
\alpha_{0}
, and
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma)\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1))\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{line}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
;

\bullet

\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W})\subset\widetilde{W}_{\text{\it reg}}
,
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W})=\tilde{\alpha}(\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma))\cong\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and
\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}
is the blow-up of the smooth point
\alpha_{0}(C)\in W_{0}
;

\bullet

\xi
and
\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle W}
are SQM’s.

Lemma 2.19
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}\geq 7
and let
D,E
be adjacent fixed prime divisors as in Lemma
2.17
(ii)
or
(iii)
. Then
we have a diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{{X_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{0}}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle W}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}}
\textstyle{{W_{0}}}

where:

\bullet

\alpha
,
\tilde{\alpha}
, and
\alpha_{0}
are divisorial elementary contractions of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
E
or its transforms;

\bullet

\xi
and
\sigma
are as in Th.-Def.
2.16

(a)
for
D
;

\bullet

\xi_{1}
is a sequence of
D
-negative and
K
-negative flips, and
E\subset\operatorname{dom}(\xi_{1})
;

\bullet

\xi_{2}
is the flip of the small extremal ray generated by
[C_{L_{0}}]
(see Lemma
2.17
);

\bullet

\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}
is a divisorial elementary contraction with
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W})
the transform of
D
, and
\operatorname{Exc}(\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W})\subset\widetilde{W}_{\text{\it reg}}
;

\bullet

\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle W}
is a SQM;

\bullet

if
D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
and
\sigma
blows-up a curve
\Gamma\subset X_{0}
, then
\Gamma\cdot\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha_{0})>0
, and

\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
and blows-up the curve
\alpha_{0}(\Gamma)\subset W_{0}
;

\bullet

if
\sigma
is of type
(3,0)^{Q}
, then

\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}
is of type
(3,0)^{\text{\it sm}}
.

Proof.

We order the sequence of
D
-negative flips in
\xi
by performing first all
E
-trivial flips; by Lemma
2.17
the loci of these flips are exceptional planes disjoint from
E
, so that
E\subset X
and its transform
\widetilde{E}\subset\widetilde{X}
are contained in the open subsets where
\xi_{1}
is an isomorphism.
Then
\operatorname{NE}(\widetilde{X})
must have a unique
\widetilde{E}
-negative extremal ray, which gives the contraction
\tilde{\alpha}\colon\widetilde{X}\to\widetilde{W}
of type
(3,2)
.

The transform
\widetilde{D}\subset\widetilde{X}
of
D
contains exceptional planes
L^{1}_{0}:=L_{0},L_{0}^{2},\dotsc,L_{0}^{d}
such that
C_{L_{0}^{i}}\equiv C_{L_{0}}
for every
i=1,\dotsc,d
, and they are the indeterminacy locus of the last flip
\xi_{2}
. Moreover
(\xi_{2})_{|\widetilde{D}}
is regular, and
(\xi_{2})_{|\widetilde{D}}\colon\widetilde{D}\to\widehat{D}
is the blow-up of
d
smooth points, with exceptional divisors
L_{0}^{1},\dotsc,L_{0}^{d}
.

If
D
is of type
(3,1)^{\text{\it sm}}
, then
\widehat{D}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over a curve
\Gamma
, and the composite map
\widetilde{D}\to\Gamma
has
d
singular fibers given by
L_{0}^{i}\cup S_{i}
, where
S_{i}\cong\mathbb{F}_{1}
is the transform of the corresponding fiber of
\widehat{D}\to\Gamma
. Moreover

\widetilde{E}\cap\widetilde{D}=S_{1}\cup\cdots\cup S_{d}

and
(\tilde{\alpha})_{|\widetilde{D}}\colon\widetilde{D}\to\tilde{\alpha}(\widetilde{D})
contracts
S_{i}
to curves, and
\tilde{\alpha}(\widetilde{D})
is another
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle over
\Gamma
. There are exceptional lines
\ell_{0}^{i}\subset\widehat{X}
corresponding to
L_{0}^{i}
(see Lemma
2.13
(a)
), and if
\widehat{E}\subset\widehat{X}
is the transform of
E
, we have
\widehat{E}\cdot\ell_{0}^{i}=-1
. In
X_{0}
, the images
\sigma(\ell_{0}^{i})
for
i=1,\dotsc,d
are the fibers of
\alpha_{0}
that meet
\Gamma
.

If
D
is of type
(3,0)^{Q}
, then
d=1
by
[
Cas22
, Prop. 6.4 and its proof]
, and

\widetilde{D}\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pt}}\widehat{D}\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{conic}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
.
If
\widehat{D}
is a smooth quadric, then
the conic is smooth, and
\widetilde{E}\cap\widetilde{D}\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}
.
If
\widehat{D}
is the cone over
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, then the conic is reducible, and
\widetilde{E}\cap\widetilde{D}
has two irreducible components, both isomorphic to
\mathbb{F}_{1}
. Finally
\alpha_{0}(\widetilde{D})\cong\mathbb{P}^{3}
.
∎

3.
Special rational contractions

In this section we introduce special contractions and rational contractions. First in §
3.1
we consider
K
-negative special contractions
f\colon X\to Y
where
X
is a smooth projective
4
-fold and
\dim Y=3
. We recall some results from
[
Cas20
]
, in particular concerning the discriminant divisor
\Delta\subset Y
, and define the
intrinsic discriminant

\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}
of
f
as the union of the irreducible components
\Delta_{0}
of
\Delta
such that
f^{*}(\Delta_{0})
is irreducible.

Using the classification of the possible
2
-dimensional fibers of
f
by Andreatta-Wiśniewski and Kachi, we show that if
y_{0}\in Y
is a singular point, then either
y_{0}\in\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}
, or
y_{0}
is a node and
f^{-1}(y_{0})
is the union of two copies of
\mathbb{P}^{2}
meeting transversally at one point, and the lines in the two
\mathbb{P}^{2}
’s are numerically equivalent in
X
(Th.
3.7
). We will use this in Section
5
to relate (in our setting) the presence of these special fibers over nodes to the non-rationality of
Y
(Lemma
5.25
).

Then in §
3.2
we turn to special
rational
contractions, and present the results needed to show that if a Fano
4
-fold
X
has a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold and
\delta_{X}\leq 1
, then there is also a special rational contraction
X\dasharrow Y
with
\dim Y=3
and
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}\in\{1,2\}
(Prop.
3.11
and Lemma
3.12
).

3.1.
Special,
K
-negative contractions from a
4
-fold to a
3
-fold

Definition 3.1
.

Let
X
be a normal and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial projective variety, and a Mori dream space.
A contraction of fiber type
f\colon X\to Y
is special if
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and, for every prime divisor
D\subset X
, either
f(D)=Y
, or
f(D)
is a prime divisor in
Y
.

Equivalently, when
\dim X=4
and
\dim Y=3
, a contraction
f\colon X\to Y
is
special
if
Y
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and
f
has at most isolated
2
-dimensional fibers and no
3
-dimensional fiber.

Special contractions of Mori dream spaces were introduced and studied in
[
Cas20
]
, to which we refer the interested reader for more details.

Theorem 3.2

(
[
Cas20
]
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective
4
-fold and a Mori dream space, and let
f\colon X\to Y
be a special,
K
-negative contraction with
\dim Y=3
. Set
m:=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}-1
. We have the following:

(a)

Y
can have at most isolated, locally factorial, canonical singularities, contained in the images of the
2
-dimensional fibers of
f
;

(b)

there are
m
prime divisors
B_{1},\dotsc,B_{m}\subset Y
such that
f^{*}B_{i}
is reducible for every
i=1,\dotsc,m
, and
f^{*}D
is irreducible for every prime divisor
D
different from
B_{1},\dotsc,B_{m}
;

(c)

B_{1},\dotsc,B_{m}
are pairwise disjoint;

(d)

f^{*}B_{i}
has two irreducible components
E_{i}
and
\widehat{E}_{i}
.
The general fiber of
f
over
B_{i}
is
e_{i}+\hat{e}_{i}
, where
e_{i},\hat{e}_{i}
are integral curves with
E_{i}\cdot e_{i}<0
,
\widehat{E}_{i}\cdot\hat{e}_{i}<0
, and
-K_{X}\cdot e_{i}=-K_{X}\cdot\hat{e}_{i}=1
, for every
i=1,\dotsc,m
.

Proof.

Statement
(a)
follows from
[
Cas20
, Prop. 2.20]
and Th.
2.11
, and the other statements from

[
Cas20
, Lemmas 3.4 and 3.5]
.
∎

3.3
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective
4
-fold and a Mori dream space, and
f\colon X\to Y
a special,
K
-negative contraction with
\dim Y=3
, as in Th.
3.2
.

If
F_{1},\dotsc,F_{r}\subset X
are the
2
-dimensional fibers of
f
, and
X_{0}:=X\smallsetminus(F_{1}\cup\cdots\cup F_{r})
, then

f_{|X_{0}}\colon X_{0}\to Y\smallsetminus(f(F_{1})\cup\cdots\cup f(F_{r}))
is a conic bundle (see Th.
2.11
).
We denote by
\Delta\subset Y
the closure of the discriminant divisor of
f_{|X_{0}}
, and we still refer to
\Delta
as the
discriminant divisor of
f
. Note that
f(F_{i})
may or may not be in
\Delta
.

By Th.
3.2
(d)
,
B_{1},\dotsc,B_{m}
are irreducible components of the discriminant divisor
\Delta
.
We define the
intrinsic discriminant divisor

\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}
of
f
to be the union of the irreducible components of
\Delta
different from
B_{1},\dotsc,B_{m}
, namely a component
\Delta_{0}
of the discriminant divisor is in
\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}
if and only if
f^{*}\Delta_{0}
is irreducible. We have
\Delta=\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}
if and only if
m=0
, equivalently
f
is elementary.

Lemma 3.4
.

In the setting of
3.3
, for every
i=1,\dotsc,m

we have
B_{i}\cap\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}=\emptyset
,
B_{i}
is a connected component of
\Delta
,
and
B_{i}
is smooth outside the
images of the
2
-dimensional fibers.

Proof.

Let
i\in\{1,\dotsc,m\}
. Outside the
2
-dimensional fibers,
f
is a conic bundle, and
B_{i}
is an irreducible component of the discriminant divisor. Since
X
is smooth, where
f
is a conic bundle, the singularities of the discriminant divisor correspond to double lines (see
[
Bea77
, Prop. 1.2]
). On the other hand there cannot be a double line
F
over
B_{i}
, because
if
\Gamma=F_{\text{\it red}}
, we would have
\Gamma\subset E_{i}\cap\widehat{E}_{i}
and
\Gamma\equiv e_{i}
,
\Gamma\equiv\hat{e}_{i}
, but
e_{i}\not\equiv\hat{e}_{i}
by
Th.
3.2
(d)
. Thus
B_{i}
is smooth outside the images of the
2
-dimensional fibers and cannot meet other irreducible components of the discriminant divisor (note that, being
Y
locally factorial, two prime divisors cannot intersect in finitely many points).
∎

Remark 3.5
.

In the setting of
3.3
, let
F:=f^{-1}(y)
be a fiber of
f
. Then
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)>1
if and only if
y\in B_{1}\cup\cdots\cup B_{m}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
y\in B_{1}
. By Th.
3.2
(d)
,
F\cap E_{1}
must contain a one-cycle
\Gamma_{1}
which is a degeneration of the curve
e_{1}
, in particular
\Gamma_{1}\equiv e_{1}
; similarly
F\cap\widehat{E}_{1}
contains a one cycle
\widehat{\Gamma}_{1}
numerically equivalent to
\hat{e}_{1}
. Therefore
[e_{1}],[\hat{e}_{1}]\in\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)
, and this classes are linearly independent because
E_{1}\cdot e_{1}<0
while
E_{1}\cdot\hat{e}_{1}\geq 0
. We conclude that

\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)>1
.

Conversely if
y\not\in B_{1}\cup\cdots\cup B_{m}
, then

F\cap E_{i}=\emptyset
for
i=1,\dotsc,m
, hence

\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)\subseteq(\ker f_{*})\cap E_{1}^{\perp}\cap\cdots\cap E_{m}^{\perp}
, where
f_{*}\colon\mathcal{N}_{1}(X)\to\mathcal{N}_{1}(Y)
is the pushforward (see Rem.
2.2
).
Moreover
\ker f_{*}
is generated by the classes
[e_{1}],\dotsc,[e_{m}],[F_{0}]
where
F_{0}
is a general fiber of
f
, and one can easily check that
(\ker f_{*})\cap E_{1}^{\perp}\cap\cdots\cap E_{m}^{\perp}=\mathbb{R}[F_{0}]
.
We conclude that
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=1
.
∎

We recall that
\mathbb{P}^{2}\bullet\mathbb{P}^{2}
is the union of two planes in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
intersecting in one point.

Lemma 3.6

(
[
Kac97
]
)
.

In the setting of
3.3
,
let
F:=f^{-1}(y_{0})
be a fiber such that
F\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}\bullet\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Then
y_{0}\not\in\Delta
and
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=1
.

Proof.

The fact that
y_{0}\not\in\Delta
follows from
[
Kac97
, Th. 3.1]
; note that, in the terminology of
[
Kac97
]
, a limit conic in
F=F_{1}\cup F_{2}
is a union of a line in
F_{1}
and a line in
F_{2}
, both containing the point
F_{1}\cap F_{2}
; hence
F
is not connected by limit conics, because for general
x,y\in F_{1}
there is no limit conic containing both of them. We also note that,
even if in
[
Kac97
]
the contraction
f
is assumed to be elementary, the proof of Th. 3.1 is local around
F
, and only needs that
F
is an isolated
2
-dimensional fiber.

Finally
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=1
follows from Rem.
3.5
.
∎

Theorem 3.7

(
[
AW98
,
Kac97
]
)
.

In the setting of
3.3
,
let
F:=f^{-1}(y_{0})
be a
2
-dimensional fiber of
f
.
Then one of the following holds:

(i)

Y
is smooth at
y_{0}
;

(ii)

\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=1
and
y_{0}\in\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}
;

(iii)

\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=1
,
F\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}\bullet\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
Y
has a node at
y_{0}
, and
y_{0}\not\in\Delta
.

Proof.

If
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=1
, then there is
an open neighborhood
Y_{0}
of
y_{0}
such that, if
X_{0}:=f^{-1}(Y_{0})
, then
f_{|X_{0}}\colon X_{0}\to Y_{0}
is elementary, and we apply the results in

[
Kac97
]
. In particular we see that for type A
[
Kac97
, Th. 0.6]
we have
(i)
; for type B
[
Kac97
, Th. 0.7]
we have
(i)
or
(iii)
; for type C
[
Kac97
, Th. 0.8]
we have
y_{0}\in\Delta
, and then
(ii)
by Rem.
3.5
.

Suppose instead that
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)>1
. The possible
F
are classified in
[
AW98
, Prop. 4.11]
, and moreover
F\not\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}\bullet\mathbb{P}^{2}
by Lemma
3.6
,
thus we have the possibilities:

\mathbb{F}_{1}
,
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1})
, or
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cup\mathbb{F}_{1}
, where in the two reducible cases, the components meet along a curve which is a line in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
and a line of the ruling in
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, or the
(-1)
-curve in
\mathbb{F}_{1}
.

In all cases we have
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(F,X)=2
and
F
does not contain exceptional planes, so we can choose
an open neighborhood
Y_{0}
of
y_{0}
such that, if
X_{0}:=f^{-1}(Y_{0})
, then
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(X_{0}/Y_{0})=2
, and no fiber of
f
over
Y_{0}
contains an exceptional plane. Set
f_{0}:=f_{|X_{0}}\colon X_{0}\to Y_{0}
, and
let us consider a factorization of
f_{0}
in elementary steps:

X_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}W_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y_{0}.

If
\alpha
is of fiber type, then
\dim W_{0}=3
and
\beta
is birational. However
\beta
cannot be small because
Y
is locally factorial (Th.
3.2
(a)
), and it cannot be divisorial otherwise there would be a divisor in
X_{0}
sent by
f_{0}
to a closed subset of codimension
\geq 2
in
Y_{0}
, impossible because
f
is special. Therefore
\alpha
is birational and
\dim W_{0}=4
.

Since
f
is
K
-negative,
\alpha
is
K
-negative too. Hence
\alpha
cannot be small, otherwise
\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha)

would be a union of exceptional planes contained in fibers of
f_{0}
(see Th.
2.8
).
Then
\alpha
is divisorial,
\dim\alpha(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha))\leq 2
and
\dim f_{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha))\leq 2
. On the other hand
f
is special, so

f_{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha))
must be a divisor in
Y_{0}
. We conclude that
\alpha
is
of type
(3,2)
, and
f_{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha))=Y_{0}\cap B_{i}
for some
i
.

We also note that the general fiber
\Gamma
of
\beta
is a curve disjoint from
\alpha(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha))
, and
f
is
K
-negative, thus
K_{W_{0}}\cdot\Gamma<0
. Since
\dim\mathcal{N}_{1}(W_{0}/Y_{0})=1
, this means that
\beta
is
K
-negative.

We have

F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}\alpha(F)=\beta^{-1}(y_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta}}{{\longrightarrow}}y_{0}

and
y_{0}\in f_{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha))

by Rem.
3.5
, thus
F\cap\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha)\neq\emptyset
, and
\alpha_{|F}
is not an isomorphism.

If
F
is irreducible, then
F\cong\mathbb{F}_{1}
or
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and
\alpha_{|F}
is either a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle, or the contraction of the
(-1)
-curve in
\mathbb{F}_{1}
. Up to exchanging this factorization of
f_{0}
with the other one, we can assume that
\alpha_{|F}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle. Then
\alpha
has one-dimensional fibers over
\alpha(F)
, therefore

\alpha(F)\subset(W_{0})_{\text{\it reg}}
by Th.
2.6
. Moreover

\alpha(F)=\beta^{-1}(y_{0})
is a one-dimensional fiber of
\beta
,
and we have
(i)
by Th.
2.11
.

If instead
F
is reducible, then
F=F_{1}\cup F_{2}
with
F_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
F_{2}\cong\mathbb{F}_{1}
or
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Again
up to exchanging this factorization of
f_{0}
with the other one, we can assume that
\alpha_{|F_{1}}
is an isomorphism and
\alpha_{|F_{2}}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle, so that
\alpha(F)\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and

\alpha(F)\cap\alpha(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha))
is a curve.
Again

\alpha
has one-dimensional fibers over
\alpha(F)\cap\alpha(\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha))
, hence

\alpha(F)\subset(W_{0})_{\text{\it reg}}
by Th.
2.6
. In this case
\alpha(F)=\beta^{-1}(y_{0})\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a
2
-dimensional fiber of
\beta
,
and we get
(i)
by
[
AW98
, Th. 5.9.6]
.
∎

Example 3.8
.

We give an example of a smooth Fano
4
-fold
X
with
\rho_{X}=2
with an elementary,
K
-negative contraction
f\colon X\to Y
with
\dim Y=3
, where
Y
has 10 nodes
y_{i}
,

f^{-1}(y_{i})\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}\bullet\mathbb{P}^{2}
for every
i
, and
f
is smooth with fiber
\mathbb{P}^{1}
over
Y\smallsetminus\{y_{1},\dotsc,y_{10}\}
, so that
\Delta=\emptyset
.
The resolution of the base
Y
is the well-known Artin and Mumford’s
3
-fold
[
AM72
]
;
we outline the description of
X
and refer the reader to

[
Bea83
, §9]
for more details.

Let
G
be the grassmannian of lines in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and
R\subset G

the subvariety given by the lines contained in a fixed pencil of quadrics in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
. Then

R
is an Enriques surface, called
Reye congruence.
Let
\sigma\colon X\to G
be the blow-up along
R
; then
X
is a smooth projective
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}=2
. Moreover there is an elementary contraction
f\colon X\to Y
, where
Y
is a locally factorial
3
-fold with precisely
10
nodes, and
f
is smooth with fiber
\mathbb{P}^{1}
outside the nodes. In particular
f
and
\sigma
are
K
-negative, and
X
is Fano.
The fibers of
f
over the nodes are isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{2}\bullet\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Remark 3.9
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a conic bundle where
Y
is smooth, quasi-projective and
X
has at most isolated singularities, and let
\Delta\subset Y
be the discriminant divisor.

Let
p\in\Delta
be such that
f^{-1}(p)
has two components, and let
q\in f^{-1}(p)
be the singular point of the fiber.
Then
X
is singular at
q
if and only if
\Delta
is singular at
p
.

Proof.

The statement being local on
Y
, we can assume that
Y
is affine and that
X\subset Y\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
is defined by the equation

F=\sum_{i,j=0}^{2}a_{ij}(y)x_{i}x_{j}=0,

where
y=(y_{j})
are local coordinates on
Y
at
p
,
a_{i}\in\mathcal{O}(Y)
, and
(x_{0}:x_{1}:x_{2})
are coordinates on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. We can also assume that
f^{-1}(p)
has equation
x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}=0
, so that
a_{11}(p)=a_{22}(p)=1
and
a_{ij}(p)=0
for all other indices. Then
f^{-1}(p)
is singular at
q_{0}=(1:0:0)
, and
q=(p,q_{0})
.

We have
\frac{\partial F}{\partial x_{i}}(q)=0
for
i=0,1,2
, and
\frac{\partial F}{\partial y_{j}}(q)=\frac{\partial a_{00}}{\partial y_{j}}(p)
for every
j
.

On the other hand, the local equation of
\Delta
at
p
is given by
D=\det(a_{ij})
. The determinant is given by a sum (with signs) of products of three elements of the matrix
(a_{ij})
lying in different rows and columns. Each such product can be expressed as a product of two functions vanishing at
p
, except
a_{00}a_{11}a_{22}
. We conclude that

\frac{\partial D}{\partial y_{j}}(p)=\frac{\partial(a_{00}a_{11}a_{22})}{\partial y_{j}}(p)=\frac{\partial a_{00}}{\partial y_{j}}(p)=\frac{\partial F}{\partial y_{j}}(q)

and the statement follows.
See also
[
Bea77
, Prop. 1.2]
.
∎

3.2.
Special rational contractions from a Fano
4
-fold to a
3
-fold

Definition 3.10
.

Let
X
be a normal and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial projective variety, and a Mori dream space.
A rational contraction of fiber type
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
is
special

if there is a SQM
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}
such that the composition
f\circ\xi^{-1}\colon\widetilde{X}\to Y
is regular and special; this does not depend on the choice of the SQM
\xi
.

We will need the following properties.

Proposition 3.11

(
[
Cas20
]
, Prop. 2.13)
.

Let
X
be a normal and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial projective variety, and a Mori dream space. Let
f\colon X\dasharrow Y
be a rational contraction of fiber type. Then
f
can be factored as
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{\prime}}}{{\dasharrow}}Y^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}Y
where
f^{\prime}
is a special rational contraction and
g
is birational.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold and
X\dasharrow Y
a special rational contraction with
\dim Y=3
.
If
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}\geq 3
, then
\delta_{X}\geq\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}-1\geq 2
.

Proof.

The argument is similar to
[
Rom19a
, proof of Lemma 3.10]
.
We consider a factorization

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\dasharrow}}\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y

where
\xi
is a SQM and
f
is a
K
-negative special contraction (see Lemma
2.14
),
and set
m:=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}-1
. By Th.
3.2

there are pairwise disjoint prime divisors
B_{1},\dotsc,B_{m}\subset Y
such that
f^{*}(B_{i})
has two irreducible components
E_{i}
and
\widehat{E}_{i}
. Moreover the general fiber of
f
over
B_{i}
is
e_{i}+\hat{e}_{i}
where
E_{i}\cdot e_{i}<0
,
\widehat{E}_{i}\cdot\hat{e}_{i}<0
, and
-K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot e_{i}=-K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot\hat{e}_{i}=1
.

We show that
E_{i}
and
\widehat{E}_{i}
are covered by irreducible curves of anticanonical degree one. Indeed if
p\in E_{i}
, there must be an effective one-cycle
\Gamma
which is a degeneration of
e_{i}
and containing
p
in its support. Then
\Gamma\equiv e_{i}
, thus every irreducible component of
\Gamma
is contracted by
f
, which is
K
-negative. Since
-K_{\widetilde{X}}\cdot\Gamma=1
,
\Gamma
must be an integral curve. Similarly for
\widehat{E}_{i}
.

By Lemma
2.13
(c)
this implies that
E_{i}\cup\widehat{E}_{i}\subset\operatorname{dom}(\xi^{-1})
. Let
E_{i}^{\prime},\widehat{E}_{i}^{\prime}\subset X
be the transforms
of
E_{i},\widehat{E}_{i}
respectively; we have
(E_{i}^{\prime}\cup\widehat{E}_{i}^{\prime})\cap(E_{j}^{\prime}\cup\widehat{E}^{\prime}_{j})=\emptyset
for every
i\neq j
.

Since
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}\geq 3
, we have

m\geq 2
.
The divisor
E^{\prime}_{1}
is disjoint from
E^{\prime}_{2},\dotsc,E^{\prime}_{m},\widehat{E}^{\prime}_{m}
, thus

\mathcal{N}_{1}(E^{\prime}_{1},X)\subseteq(E_{2}^{\prime})^{\perp}\cap\cdots\cap(E_{m}^{\prime})^{\perp}\cap(\widehat{E}_{m}^{\prime})^{\perp}

(see Rem.
2.2
).
Moreover intersecting with (the transforms of) the curves
e_{i}
,
\hat{e}_{m}
we see that the classes
[E^{\prime}_{2}],\dotsc,[E^{\prime}_{m}],[\widehat{E}^{\prime}_{m}]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(X)
are linearly independent, and this gives
\operatorname{codim}\mathcal{N}_{1}(E^{\prime}_{1},X)\geq m
, hence
\delta_{X}\geq m=\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}-1
.
∎

4.
Weak Fano
3
-folds

We recall that a normal and
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial projective variety
Y
is
weak Fano
if
-K_{Y}
is nef and big. This is an auxiliary
section where we present some results on Fano and weak Fano
3
-folds
Y
with locally factorial and canonical singularities; in particular we are interested in bounding
-K_{Y}^{3}
. These results will be applied in the rest of paper to study the base of a special rational contraction
X\dasharrow Y
where
X
is a Fano
4
-fold and
\dim Y=3
. The reader may skip this section and come back to it when needed.

Let
Y
be a weak Fano
3
-fold with locally factorial, canonical singularities. Then
Y
is log Fano, and
|-mK_{Y}|
for
m\gg 0
defines a birational map
\varphi\colon Y\to Z
, that we call the
anticanonical map
of
Y
. Moreover
Z
is a Gorenstein Fano
3
-fold, the
anticanonical model
of
Y
.

Lemma 4.1

(
[
Pro05
,
Kar09
]
)
.

Let
Y
be a Fano
3
-fold with at most locally factorial and canonical singularities.
Then
-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 64
.

Proof.

We use the results in
[
Pro05
,
Kar09
]
on the anticanonical degree of Fano
3
-folds with Gorenstein canonical singularities.

By
[
Pro05
, Th. 1.5]
we have
-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 72
, and if
-K_{Y}^{3}=72

then
Y
should be

\mathbb{P}(1,1,1,3)
or
\mathbb{P}(1,1,4,6)
, but these two varieties are not locally factorial.

Then
[
Kar09
, Th. 1.5]
shows that, if
64<-K_{Y}^{3}<72
, then there are two possibilities for
Y
, with
-K_{Y}^{3}=66
or
-K_{Y}^{3}=70
.

If
-K_{Y}^{3}=70
, then by
[
Kar09
, §3, in particular p. 1226]
there is a birational morphism
\tau\colon W\to Y
with
\operatorname{Exc}(\tau)
an irreducible curve, which is contracted to the unique singular point of
Y
. Then
Y
is not even
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, indeed let
H\subset W
be a general very ample divisor, so that
H
intersects
\operatorname{Exc}(\tau)
in points. Then
\tau(H)\subset Y
is a prime Weil divisor which cannot be
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier, because
\tau^{-1}(\tau(H))=H\cup\operatorname{Exc}(\tau)
is not a divisor, hence the pullback of
m\tau(H)
does not exist for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

If
-K_{Y}^{3}=66
, then by
[
Kar09
, Th. 1.5, Prop. 5.2]

Y
is toric and singular, thus again
Y
cannot be locally factorial.
∎

Lemma 4.2

(
[
Ou18
]
)
.

Let
Y
be a Fano
3
-fold with at most isolated, locally factorial, and canonical singularities.
Assume also that
-K_{Y}^{3}>24
,
\rho_{Y}=2
, and that
Y
has two distinct elementary contractions of fiber type. Then
Y
is smooth and rational, and one of the following holds:

(i)

Y\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=54
;

(ii)

Y\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(T_{\mathbb{P}^{2}})
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=48
;

(iii)

Y\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a divisor of degree
(1,2)
, and
-K_{Y}^{3}=30
.

Proof.

Without the assumption on
-K_{Y}^{3}
, the possible
Y
’s are classified in

[
Ou18
, Th. 1.2]
; there are
7
families, and they are all degenerations of smooth Fano
3
-folds. By checking the anticanonical degree we get the statement. Note that in case
(iii)
, the first projection from
\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
realizes
Y
as a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
\mathbb{P}^{2}
, thus
Y
is smooth and rational.
∎

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
Y
be a weak Fano
3
-fold, not Fano, with at most isolated, canonical, and locally factorial singularities. Assume also that
\rho_{Y}=2
, that
Y
has two distinct elementary rational contractions of fiber type, and that the anticanonical map of
Y
is small with exceptional locus contained in
Y_{\text{\it reg}}
. Then one of the following holds:

(i)

Y\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 2})
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=54
;

(ii)

up to flops,
Y
is as in
[
JP08
, Th. 3.6(1)]
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=40
;

(iii)

-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 32
.

Before proving this proposition, we need some auxiliary results. The techniques used below are standard in the study of weak Fano threefolds, see for instance
[
IP99
,
JP08
,
JPR11
,
Pro05
,
Pro23
]
.

Let
Y
be as in Prop.
4.3
, and set
Y_{1}:=Y
.
Since
\rho_{Y_{1}}=2
and
-K_{Y_{1}}
is nef and big but not ample,
Y_{1}
has two elementary contractions, one
g_{1}\colon Y_{1}\to W_{1}
which is
K
-negative, and another one which is
K
-trivial. This last one is the anticanonical map
\varphi\colon Y_{1}\to Z
, so it is small by assumption, with exceptional locus contained in
(Y_{1})_{\text{\it reg}}
; let
\xi\colon Y_{1}\dasharrow Y_{2}
be its flop. Then
Y_{2}
is still weak Fano, and since terminal flops preserve the singularity type (see
[
KM98
, Th. 6.15]
), also the indeterminacy locus of
\xi^{-1}
is contained in
(Y_{2})_{\text{\it reg}}
, and
Y_{2}
has the same singularities as
Y_{1}
; note that
-K_{Y_{1}}^{3}=-K_{Y_{2}}^{3}
. The second elementary contraction
g_{2}\colon Y_{2}\to W_{2}
is
K
-negative.

\textstyle{{Y_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{Y_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{2}}
\textstyle{W_{1}}
\textstyle{{W_{2}}}

We note that
g_{i}
cannot be small by Th.
2.7
, therefore
\operatorname{Mov}(Y_{1})=\operatorname{Nef}(Y_{1})\cup\xi^{*}(\operatorname{Nef}(Y_{2}))
.
Since
Y_{1}
has two elementary rational contractions of fiber type, these must be
g_{1}
and
g_{2}\circ\xi
, namely
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
must be of fiber type.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. If
\dim W_{i}=1
, then
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
g_{i}
is a fibration in del Pezzo surfaces.
If
\dim W_{i}=2
, then the surface
W_{i}
is smooth (see
[
Ou18
, Lemma 5.5]
), and it is rational with
\rho_{W_{i}}=1
, thus
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
; in this case
g_{i}
is a conic bundle (see Prop.
2.12
).

Let
D_{i}\subset Y_{i}
be a general fiber of
g_{i}
if
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, or the pullback of a general line if
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Let also

\widetilde{D}_{2}\subset Y_{1}
be the transform of
D_{2}\subset Y_{2}
.
Then
[D_{1}],[\widetilde{D}_{2}]\in\mathcal{N}^{1}(Y_{1})
generate the cone of effective divisors, and since
Y_{1}
is locally factorial, we have

(4.4)

m(-K_{Y_{1}})=a_{1}D_{1}+a_{2}\widetilde{D}_{2}

with
m,a_{1},a_{2}
positive integers such that
\gcd(m,a_{1},a_{2})=1
.

Lemma 4.5
.

If
W_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
D_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, then

Y_{1}\cong Y_{2}\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 2})
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=54
.

Proof.

We have
Y_{1}=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{F})
where
\mathcal{F}
is a rank
3
vector bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, because
Y_{1}
is locally factorial (see
[
HN13
, Rem. 3.2]
and references therein); moreover
\mathcal{F}
is decomposable. Since
Y_{1}
is weak Fano, not Fano, and it has small anticanonical map, the only possibility is
Y_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 2})
. Then
-K_{Y_{1}}^{3}=54
and
Y_{2}\cong Y_{1}
, and we have the statement.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

If
W_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
g_{1}
is smooth, then one of the following holds:

(i)

Y
is as in
[
JP08
, Th. 3.6(1)]
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=40
;

(ii)

-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 24
.

Proof.

We have
Y=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\mathcal{F})
with
\mathcal{F}
a rank
2
vector bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. The possible
\mathcal{F}
such that
Y
is weak Fano have been classified in
[
Lan98
]
when
\mathcal{F}
has odd degree, and
[
Yas12
]
when
\mathcal{F}
has even degree.

When
\mathcal{F}
has even degree, we can assume that
c_{1}(\mathcal{F})=0
, and since
Y
is not Fano, by
[
Yas12
, Prop. 2.10 and 2.11]
we have
c_{2}(\mathcal{F})\in\{4,5,6\}
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=54-8c_{2}(\mathcal{F})\in\{6,14,22\}
.

When
\mathcal{F}
has odd degree, we can assume that
c_{1}(\mathcal{F})=-1
, then
-K_{Y}^{3}=8(7-c_{2}(\mathcal{F}))
. The possibilities for
\mathcal{F}
are given in
[
Lan98
, Th. 3.2]
; moreover
in this case

Y=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(\mathcal{F})
has automatically index
2
2

2

The index of a weak Fano variety is the divisibility of
-K
in the Picard group, as for Fano varieties.
2; when the anticanonical map is small
these threefolds have been classified also in
[
JP08
, Th. 3.6]
.

Let us assume that
-K_{Y}^{3}>24
, equivalently that
c_{2}(\mathcal{F})<4
.
Since
Y
is not Fano, by
[
Lan98
, Th. 3.2]
we have

c_{2}(\mathcal{F})\in\{-2,1,2,3\}
.
Moreover, since the anticanonical map is small, we have

c_{2}(\mathcal{F})\in\{2,3\}
by
[
JP08
, Th. 3.6]
.
If
c_{2}(\mathcal{F})=2
then
-K_{Y}^{3}=40
and
Y
is as in
[
Lan98
, Th. 3.2.5]
, which is the same as
[
JP08
, Th. 3.6(1)]
(and also
[
JPR11
, 2.13(iv)]
). Finally, if

c_{2}(\mathcal{F})=3
,
Y
should be as in
[
JP08
, Th. 3.6(2)]
but in this case
g_{2}\colon Y_{2}\to W_{2}
is birational, which is excluded by our assumptions.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Suppose that
g_{i}
contracts some irreducible curve
\Gamma_{i}\subset Y_{i}
with
-K_{Y_{i}}\cdot\Gamma_{i}=1
for
i=1,2
.
Then
-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 24
.

Proof.

Intersecting (
4.4
) with
\Gamma_{1}
we get
m=a_{2}b
with
b:=\widetilde{D}_{2}\cdot\Gamma_{1}
, thus
a_{2}(b(-K_{Y_{1}})-\widetilde{D}_{2})\sim a_{1}D_{1}
. This implies that
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{1}}(b(-K_{Y_{1}})-\widetilde{D}_{2})\in(g_{1})^{*}\operatorname{Pic}(W_{1})=\mathbb{Z}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{1}}(D_{1})
(see
[
KM98
, Th. 3.7(4)]
), therefore
a_{2}|a_{1}
. Working in
Y_{2}
and intersecting with
\Gamma_{2}
, we get
a_{1}|a_{2}
and hence
a_{1}=a_{2}
. On the other hand
a_{2}|m
and
\gcd(a_{2},m)=1
, and we conclude that
a_{1}=a_{2}=1
and
m(-K_{Y_{1}})=D_{1}+\widetilde{D}_{2}
, so that:

(4.8)

-K_{Y_{1}}^{3}\leq(-K_{Y_{1}})^{2}\cdot(-mK_{Y_{1}})=(-K_{Y_{1}})^{2}\cdot D_{1}+(-K_{Y_{1}})^{2}\cdot\widetilde{D}_{2}.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. If
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
, then
D_{i}
is a del Pezzo surface, thus
(-K_{Y_{i}})^{2}\cdot D_{i}=(-K_{D_{i}})^{2}\leq 9
.
If instead
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, then
g_{i}
is a conic bundle; the surface
D_{i}
is smooth, rational, and has a conic bundle over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with
d_{i}
singular fibers, where
d_{i}
is the degree of the discriminant divisor of
g_{i}
. An elementary computation gives

(-K_{Y_{i}})^{2}\cdot D_{i}=12-d_{i}\leq 12
.

Finally we have
(-K_{Y_{1}})^{2}\cdot\widetilde{D}_{2}=(-K_{Y_{2}})^{2}\cdot D_{2}
; this is a well-known fact for flops, that can be seen as follows. Consider a locally factorial resolution of
\xi\colon Y_{1}\dasharrow Y_{2}
:

\textstyle{{\widehat{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\textstyle{Y_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{{Y_{2}}}

We have
\alpha_{1}^{*}K_{Y_{1}}=\alpha_{2}^{*}K_{Y_{2}}
; recall that
\varphi\colon Y_{1}\to Z
is the anticanonical map. If
E\subset\widehat{Y}
is an irreducible exceptional divisor of
\alpha_{1}
, we have
(\varphi\circ\alpha_{1})(E)=\{\text{\it pt}\}
, and
\alpha_{1}^{*}K_{Y_{1}}=(\varphi\circ\alpha_{1})^{*}K_{Z}
, thus
(\alpha_{1}^{*}K_{Y_{1}})_{|E}\equiv 0
. This implies that, for every pair of divisors
B,B^{\prime}
in
Y_{1}
, if
\widetilde{B},\widetilde{B}^{\prime}\subset Y_{2}
are their transforms, we have
K_{Y_{1}}\cdot B\cdot B^{\prime}=K_{Y_{2}}\cdot\widetilde{B}\cdot\widetilde{B}^{\prime}
.

From (
4.8
)
we conclude that
-K_{Y_{1}}^{3}\leq 24
.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

Suppose that
W_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
D_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
D_{2}\not\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, and that if
W_{2}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, then
g_{2}
is not smooth.
Then
-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 32
.

Proof.

We proceed similarly to the proof of Lemma
4.7
. Let
\Gamma_{1}\subset D_{1}
be
a line in the quadric, and note that
(-K_{D_{1}})^{2}=8
. Suppose first that there exists an irreducible curve
\Gamma_{2}\subset Y_{2}
contracted by
g_{2}
such that
-K_{Y_{2}}\cdot\Gamma_{2}=1
; as before we have
(-K_{Y_{2}})^{2}\cdot D_{2}\leq 12
.
Intersecting (
4.4
) with
\Gamma_{1}
we get
2m=a_{2}b
with
b:=\widetilde{D}_{2}\cdot\Gamma_{1}
, thus
a_{2}(b(-K_{Y_{1}})-2\widetilde{D}_{2})=2a_{1}D_{1}
and
a_{2}|2a_{1}
;
intersecting with
\Gamma_{2}
we get
a_{1}|a_{2}
. This implies that either
a_{1}=a_{2}
or
2a_{1}=a_{2}
. Then using that
a_{1}|m
and
\gcd(a_{1},a_{2},m)=1
we deduce that
a_{1}=1
,

a_{2}\leq 2
, and
-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 8a_{1}+12a_{2}\leq 32
.

Suppose now
g_{2}
does not contract curves of anticanonical degree one. Then, by our assumptions, it must be
W_{2}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
D_{2}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. By taking
\Gamma_{2}
a line in the quadric
D_{2}
and proceeding as above, we get
a_{i}\leq 2
for
i=1,2
,
(-K_{D_{2}})^{2}=8
, and
-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 8a_{1}+8a_{2}\leq 32
.
∎

Proof of Prop.
4.3
.

If for some
i\in\{1,2\}
we have
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
D_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, the statement follows from Lemma
4.5
. Similarly, if for some
i\in\{1,2\}
we have
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
g_{i}
is smooth, the statement follows from Lemma
4.6
. Therefore we can assume that, for
i=1,2
, either
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
and
g_{i}
is singular, or
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
D_{i}\not\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Now if for
i=1,2

g_{i}
contracts some irreducible curve of anticanonical degree one, we apply Lemma
4.7
. Otherwise, up to switching
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
we can assume that
W_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
D_{1}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and we apply Lemma
4.9
.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

In the setting of Lemma
4.9
, suppose moreover that
Y
is smooth.
Then one of the following holds:

(i)

Y
is as in
[
JP08
, Th. 3.5(3)]
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=32
;

(ii)

-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 24
.

Proof.

Smooth weak Fano threefolds with a del Pezzo fibration, and having small anticanonical map, have been classified in
[
JPR11
]
and
[
Tak22
]
. In our setting
Y_{1}
has a quadric fibration and
g_{2}
is either a singular conic bundle, or another del Pezzo fibration. Then, by
[
Tak22
, Th. 2.3]
, if
-K_{Y}^{3}>24
the only possibility for
Y_{1}
is
[
Tak22
, (2.3.4)]
; then
Y_{1}
has index two, and it is the same as
[
JP08
, Th. 3.5(3)]
(and also
[
JPR11
, 2.13(1.iii)]
).
∎

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
Y
be a smooth weak Fano
3
-fold with
\rho_{Y}=2
and with two distinct elementary rational contractions of fiber type. Assume moreover that
Y
is not Fano, and that the anticanonical map of
Y
is small.
Then
one of the following holds:

(i)

Y\cong\mathbb{P}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\mathcal{O}\oplus\mathcal{O}(1)^{\oplus 2})
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=54
;

(ii)

up to flops
Y
is as in
[
JP08
, Th. 3.6(1)]
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=40
;

(iii)

Y
is as in
[
JP08
, Th. 3.5(3)]
and
-K_{Y}^{3}=32
;

(iv)

-K_{Y}^{3}\leq 24
.

Proof.

We proceed as in the proof of Prop.
4.3
, just applying Lemma
4.10
instead of Lemma
4.9
.
∎

5.
The case of relative Picard number two

In this section we study Fano
4
-folds having a special rational contraction onto a
3
-dimensional target, with relative Picard number two, and we prove Th.
1.11
from the Introduction; for the reader’s convenience, we report the statement here.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
X
be a smooth Fano
4
-fold that not isomorphic to a product of surfaces, and having a special rational contraction

f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}\colon X\dasharrow Y
with
\dim Y=3
and
\rho_{X}-\rho_{Y}=2
.
Then
\rho_{X}\leq 9
.

Moreover, if
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, then
X
is the blow-up of
W
along a normal surface
S
,
where
W
is the Fano model of the blow-up of
\mathbb{P}^{4}
at
\rho_{X}-2
points (see Ex.
7.2
), and
S\subset W
is the transform of a surface
A\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
containing the blown-up points, as follows:

(i)

A
is a cubic scroll;

(ii)

A
is a cone over a twisted cubic;

(iii)

A
is a sextic (singular) K3 surface, with rational double points of type
A_{1}
or
A_{2}
at the blown-up points, and
\rho_{X}=7
.

In cases
(i)
and
(iii)
the surface
S
is smooth, while in
(ii)

S
has one singular point, given by the vertex of the cone.
Moreover
Y
is smooth, and up to flops
Y\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
.

5.2
Outline of the proof.

The proof of Th.
5.1
is quite long and articulated, and it will take the whole section; let us outline the strategy. We assume that
\rho_{X}\geq 7
, so that
\rho_{Y}\geq 5
.

We consider a SQM
\xi\colon X\dasharrow\widetilde{X}
such that
f:=f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}\circ\xi^{-1}\colon\widetilde{X}\to Y
is regular and
K
-negative.
There is a unique prime divisor
B\subset Y
such that
f^{*}(B)=E_{1}+E_{2}
is reducible, and it is a connected component of the discriminant divisor of
f
(
5.5
).

We show that
f
factors as
\widetilde{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{\alpha}}}{{\to}}\widetilde{W}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}Y
, where
\tilde{\alpha}
is a divisorial elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor
E_{1}
, and that there is a divisorial elementary contraction
\alpha\colon X\to W
of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor the transform of
E_{1}
, so that there is a SQM
W\dasharrow\widetilde{W}
(see diagram (
5.3
) below).

The first part of the proof follows the same lines as
[
Cas13
,
Cas20
]
: we show that
Y
is weak Fano and that, up to flops, there is a blow-up
k\colon Y\to Y_{0}
of
r
smooth, distinct points
p_{1},\dotsc,p_{r}\in Y_{0}
, where
Y_{0}
is
a weak Fano
3
-fold
Y_{0}
with
\rho_{Y_{0}}\leq 2
(
5.12
, Lemma
5.16
).
Then we show, in sequence:

\bullet

the anticanonical map
\varphi\colon Y\to Z
of
Y
is small, and
\rho_{Z}=1
(
5.14
, Lemma
5.19
);

\bullet

-K_{Y}=\lambda B
for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
(Lemma
5.20
);

\bullet

the composition
\varphi\circ f_{\scriptscriptstyle X}\colon X\to Z
is regular and factors through
\alpha
, so it gives a contraction of fiber type
W\to Z
(
5.22
- see diagram (
5.3
) below);

\bullet

B
is the discriminant of
f
, and
\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}=\emptyset
(Lemma
5.23
, see
3.3
for
\Delta_{\text{\it intr}}
);

\bullet

Y
and
Y_{0}
are nodal (
5.24
).

Finally we show that
Y
and
Y_{0}
are smooth and rational, and thanks to the constraints given by our setting, that (up to flops) there are only six possibilities for
Y_{0}
(Lemma
5.26
). We mention here that we use the rationality of
Y
to deduce smoothness, because the fibers of our special contraction over the nodes are unions of two copies of
\mathbb{P}^{2}
intersecting transversally at one point, and the lines in the two
\mathbb{P}^{2}
’s are numerically equivalent; this gives an obstruction to rationality (Lemma
5.25
).

Then we show that
f
descends to a special contraction
f_{0}\colon X_{0}\to Y_{0}
with
\rho_{X_{0}}-\rho_{Y_{0}}=2
,
where
X\dasharrow X_{0}
is a birational (rational) contraction, and we can describe it using the classification of fixed prime divisors of
X
(Lemma
5.28
).
Also the factorization of
f
descends to a
factorization of
f_{0}
as
X_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{0}}}{{\to}}W_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{0}}}{{\to}}Y_{0}
, where
\alpha_{0}
is a divisorial elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
with exceptional divisor the transform of
E_{1}
(
5.41
). Moreover
\pi_{0}

is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle outside possibly finitely many
2
-dimensional fibers, and the fibers
\pi_{0}^{-1}(p_{i})
over the blown-up points are isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
(Lemma
5.43
).

Using the properties of fixed prime divisors in
X
, we show that the birational map
\widetilde{W}\dasharrow W_{0}
factors as a SQM
\widetilde{W}\dasharrow\widehat{W}
followed by a divisorial contraction
\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}\colon\widehat{W}\to W_{0}
, where for each
i=1,\dotsc,r

\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}
blows-up either the fiber
\pi_{0}^{-1}(p_{i})
, or a point in that fiber (Lemma
5.46
).

(5.3)

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{{X_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{0}}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{\scriptscriptstyle W}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{{\widehat{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle W}}
\textstyle{{W_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{0}}
\textstyle{Z}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{{Y_{0}}}
