Numerical invariants of the Fano
4
-folds from §
7.2

Remark 7.10
.

For
r=0
,
X_{0}
is the blow-up of
W_{0}:=\operatorname{Bl}_{q_{0}}{\mathbb{P}^{4}}
along the transform
S_{0}
of
A
. Let

\widetilde{F}_{i}\subset X_{0}
be the transform of the line
\overline{q_{0}q_{i}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
, for
i=1,\dotsc,r
, and let
\widehat{X}\to X_{0}
be the blow-up of the
r
curves
\widetilde{F}_{1},\dotsc,\widetilde{F}_{r}
.
Then
X
is a SQM of
\widehat{X}
, compare diagram (
5.47
).

7.3.
New families as in case
(b)

We keep the notation as in §
7.1
.
Let
Q\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
be a general quadric hypersurface containing
q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}
. Moreover
let
M\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
be a general cubic hypersurface with double points at
q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}
and
such that, if

A:=Q\cap M
, then
A
is a reduced and irreducible surface with
\operatorname{Sing}(A)=\{q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}\}
,
having rational double points of type
A_{1}
or
A_{2}
in
q_{i}
for every
i
;
A
is a sextic (singular) K3 surface.

Let
\widehat{Q}
and
\widehat{M}
,
Q_{W}
and
M_{W}
be the transforms of
Q
and
M
in
\widehat{W}
and
W
respectively;
then
\widehat{Q}
is contained in the open subset where the map
\widehat{W}\dasharrow W
is an isomorphism.

The transform
S\subset\widehat{W}
of
A
is a smooth K3 surface, and since
S\subset\widehat{Q}
,
S
is contained in the open subset where the map
\widehat{W}\dasharrow W
is an isomorphism.
We will show the following.

Proposition 7.11
.

The
4
-fold
X
is Fano if and only if
r\in\{0,\dotsc,4\}
; in these cases we have

\rho_{X}=r+3\in\{3,\dotsc,7\}
.

Remark 7.12
.

For
r=0
and
\rho_{X}=3
, this Fano
4
-fold is the same as
[
BFMT21
, K3-50]
.

The surface
B_{0}
is a quartic surface with double points at
p_{1},\dotsc,p_{r}
, and
B
is a smooth K3 surface with
B\in|-K_{Y}|
. We note that, by generality of
Q
and
M
,
A
does not contain lines through
q_{0},\dotsc,q_{r}
, the projection from
q_{0}
is finite on
A
, and

\pi_{|S}\colon S\to B
is an isomorphism. This also implies that every fiber of
f\colon\widetilde{X}\to Y
is one-dimensional.

Set
h:=(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{4}}(1))_{|S}
nef and big on
S
, and set
C_{i}:=\widehat{D}_{i|S}
. Note that
C_{i}
is a conic in
\widehat{D}_{i}\cong\mathbb{P}^{3}
, smooth if
q_{i}
is of type
A_{1}
for
A
, reducible if of type
A_{2}
. Moreover
C_{i}
is a
(-2)
-curve in
S
if
q_{i}
is of type
A_{1}
, the union of two
(-2)
-curves if of type
A_{2}
.

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
r\in\{0,\dotsc,4\}
. We have
\mathcal{N}_{S/W}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}(2h-\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S}(3h-2\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i})
, and
\mathcal{N}_{S/W}
is ample.

Proof.

Since
\widehat{Q}
is contained in the open subset where the map
\widehat{W}\dasharrow W
is an isomorphism, we have
S=Q_{W}\cap M_{W}
in
W
, and
\mathcal{N}_{S/W}\cong\mathcal{O}_{W}(Q_{W})_{|S}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{W}(M_{W})_{|S}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{W}}(\widehat{Q})_{|S}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{W}}(\widehat{M})_{|S}
.

We note that
M
has multiplicity
2
at each
q_{i}
, therefore

\widehat{Q}_{|S}\sim 2h-\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}
and
\widehat{M}_{|S}\sim 3h-2\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}
,
which yields the first statement.

We show ampleness of both linear summands of
\mathcal{N}_{S/W}
.
We have
-K_{\widehat{W}}=\sigma^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{4}}(5)-3\sum_{i=0}^{r}\widehat{D}_{i}
, thus

(-K_{W})_{|S}\cong(-K_{\widehat{W}})_{|S}=5h-3\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}

is ample in
S
, because
W
is Fano. Hence

3\widehat{Q}_{|S}\sim 3\Bigl(2h-\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}\Bigr)=h+\Bigl(5h-3\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}\Bigr)

is ample in
S
.

For the second summand, we treat the case
r=4
maximal, the other cases being simpler.
We recall that
W
is a toric Fano
4
-fold, thus it has an explicit combinatorial description, see
[
Bat99
, 3.5.8(iii)]
. In particular
the cone
\operatorname{NE}(W)
has 20 extremal rays, all small. The loci of 10 of these rays
R_{i}
are the exceptional planes in the indeterminacy locus of the map
{W}\dasharrow\widehat{W}
, corresponding to the exceptional lines
\ell_{ij}\subset\widehat{W}
given by the transforms of the lines
\overline{q_{i}q_{j}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
. The loci
L_{abc}
of the remaining 10 extremal rays
R_{i}^{\prime}
are exceptional planes given by the transforms of the planes
P_{abc}
spanned by
q_{a},q_{b},q_{c}
in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
. The birational map
L_{abc}\dasharrow P_{abc}
is a standard Cremona map, and a line in
L_{abc}
corresponds to a conic in
P_{abc}
through
q_{a},q_{b},q_{c}
.

Let us consider now
\mathcal{O}_{W}(M_{W})\in\operatorname{Pic}(W)
. We have
\widehat{M}\cdot\ell_{ij}=-1
in
\widehat{W}
, thus
M_{W}\cdot R_{i}>0
.
On the other hand the previous description of
L_{abc}
implies that
M_{W}\cdot R_{i}^{\prime}=0
, therefore

M_{W}
if nef in
W
, and it defines a contraction
\beta\colon W\to W_{0}

such that
\operatorname{NE}(\beta)
is generated by the 10 extremal rays
R_{i}^{\prime}
, and

\operatorname{Exc}(\beta)
contains all the
L_{abc}
.
We claim that
\beta
is birational and small, with exceptional locus equal to the union of all the
L_{abc}
. Indeed one can check that
\sum_{j=0}^{4}D_{j}\cdot R_{i}^{\prime}=-2
for every
i
, therefore if
\Gamma\subset W
is an irreducible curve contracted by
\beta
, we must have
\sum_{j=0}^{4}D_{j}\cdot\Gamma<0
and hence
\Gamma\subset D_{j}
for some
j
. We have
{D}_{j}\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{4\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and
\beta_{|D_{j}}
is precisely the blow-up of
4
points in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
, so that
\Gamma\subset L_{abc}
with
a,b,c\neq j
.

Now we show that
S_{W}\cap L_{abc}=\emptyset
for every
a,b,c
.
Set for simplicity
P:=P_{abc}\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
. Then
P\cap Q
is a conic in
P
through
q_{a},q_{b},q_{c}
, which must be smooth because the points are not aligned and, being general,
Q
does not contain any line
\overline{q_{i}q_{j}}
.
On the other hand
P\cap M=\overline{q_{a}q_{b}}\cup\overline{q_{a}q_{c}}\cup\overline{q_{b}q_{c}}
; in particular set-theoretically
P\cap A=\{q_{a},q_{b},q_{c}\}
.

Let
\widehat{P}\subset\widehat{W}
be the transform of
P
, and consider the exceptional divisor
\widehat{D}_{a}
. Then
\widehat{P}\cap\widehat{D}_{a}=\Gamma
line in
\widehat{D}_{a}\cong\mathbb{P}^{3}
, and
\widehat{M}\cap\Gamma=\{x_{b},x_{c}\}
where
x_{i}:=\widehat{D}_{a}\cap\ell_{ai}
for
i=b,c
. Moreover
\widehat{Q}\cap\Gamma
is a point, corresponding to the tangent direction to the conic
P\cap Q
at
q_{a}
. For
i=b,c
this conic contains
q_{i}
, thus
\overline{q_{a}q_{i}}
is not tangent to
P\cap Q
, and
\widehat{Q}\cap\Gamma\neq x_{i}
. We conclude that
S_{\widehat{W}}\cap\Gamma=\widehat{Q}\cap\widehat{M}\cap\Gamma=\emptyset
, namely
S_{\widehat{W}}\cap\widehat{P}\cap\widehat{D}_{a}=\emptyset
, and finally that

S_{\widehat{W}}\cap\widehat{P}=\emptyset
. Since
S
is contained in the open subset where the map
\widehat{W}\dasharrow W
is an isomorphism, we still have
S_{W}\cap L_{abc}=\emptyset
in
W
. We get
S_{W}\cap\operatorname{Exc}(\beta)=\emptyset
, therefore
(M_{W})_{|S_{W}}
is ample.
∎

We note that
T\subset\widetilde{W}
is the transform of the cone over
A
in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
with vertex
q_{0}
, and it is disjoint from the transforms of the lines
\overline{q_{i}q_{j}}
for
1\leq i<j\leq r
, thus it is contained in the open subset where
the map
\widetilde{W}\dasharrow W
is an isomorphism.

Lemma 7.14
.

For
r\in\{0,\dotsc,4\}
,
-K_{X|\widetilde{T}}
is ample.

Proof.

We have
-K_{X|\widetilde{T}}\cong-K_{\widetilde{X}|\widetilde{T}}
, thus it is enough to show that
-K_{\widetilde{X}|\widetilde{T}}
is ample.

Since
\pi_{|S}\colon S\to B
is an isomorphism, with a slight abuse of notation we still denote by
C_{i}
the image of
C_{i}\subset S
in
B
; for
i=1,\dotsc,r
we have
C_{i}=G_{i|B}
.

The K3 surface
S\cong B
has a map to
\mathbb{P}^{4}
with image the sextic
A
, and we denote by
h
the pullback of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{4}}(1)
, and a map to
\mathbb{P}^{3}
with image the quartic
B_{0}
, and we denote by
h_{0}
the pullback of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{3}}(1)
; we have

h_{0}=h-C_{0}=H_{Y|B}
.

We have
\eta_{|{S}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{H}-\sum_{i=1}^{r}\widetilde{D}_{i})_{|S}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}(h-\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})
,
and if
L:=\mathcal{O}_{B}(h-\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})\in\operatorname{Pic}(B)
, the section
S
of
\pi_{T}\colon T\to B
corresponds to a surjection
\phi\colon\mathcal{E}_{|B}\twoheadrightarrow L
. This implies that:

\mathcal{O}_{T}(S)_{|S}\cong\mathcal{N}_{{S}/{T}}\cong\ker(\phi)^{-1}\otimes L=\det(\mathcal{E}_{|B})^{-1}\otimes L^{\otimes 2},

and also that in
{T}
we have
{S}\sim\eta_{|T}+\pi_{T}^{*}(L-\det\mathcal{E}_{|B})
.

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
7.7
, we also have:

\displaystyle B_{|B}

\displaystyle\sim(-K_{Y})_{|B}=4h_{0}-2\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}=4h-4C_{0}-2\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i},

\displaystyle{\widetilde{T}}_{|\widetilde{T}}

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{T}^{*}(B)_{|B}-S=-\eta_{|T}+\pi_{T}^{*}(B_{|B}+\det\mathcal{E}_{|B}-L),

\displaystyle-K_{{T}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{T}^{*}(-\det\mathcal{E}_{|B})+2\eta_{|T},

\displaystyle-K_{\widetilde{X}|\widetilde{T}}

\displaystyle=-K_{\widetilde{T}}+\widetilde{T}_{|\widetilde{T}}\cong\eta_{|T}+\pi_{T}^{*}(B_{|B}-L)=\eta_{|T}+\pi_{T}^{*}\Bigl(3h-3C_{0}-\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}\Bigr),\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{|B}

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{O}_{B}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{B}\bigl(h_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}\bigr)=\mathcal{O}_{B}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{B}\bigl(h-\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}\bigr).

We conclude that
-K_{X|\widetilde{T}}
is isomorphic to the tautological class for

(7.15)

{T}\cong\mathbb{P}_{B}\Bigl(\mathcal{O}\bigl(3h-\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}-3C_{0}\bigr)\oplus\mathcal{O}\bigl(4h-2\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}-3C_{0}\bigr)\Bigr).

In
B
we have
h\cdot C_{0}=0
and
C_{0}^{2}=-2
, thus
h_{0}\cdot C_{0}=(h-C_{0})\cdot C_{0}=2
, and
C_{0}\subset B
is the transform of a conic
\Gamma_{0}\subset B_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
not containing any
p_{i}
; we have
\Gamma_{0}\cong C_{0}
, thus
\Gamma_{0}
is reduced, either smooth or reducible.
Let
\Pi\subset\mathbb{P}^{3}
be the plane containing
\Gamma_{0}
, so that
\Pi_{|B_{0}}=\Gamma_{0}+\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}
where
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}
is another conic in
\Pi
, and
(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime})^{2}=-2
in
B_{0}
, in particular
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}
cannot be a double line.
Moreover
p_{i}\not\in\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}
for every
i
, otherwise we would not have
K_{B}\sim 0
. Let
C_{0}^{\prime}\subset B
be the transform of
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}
.

Set
h^{\prime}:=h-C_{0}+C_{0}^{\prime}=h_{0}+C_{0}^{\prime}
in
B
.
We have
h_{0}\sim C_{0}+C_{0}^{\prime}\sim h^{\prime}-C_{0}^{\prime}
,
C_{0}\sim h^{\prime}-2C_{0}^{\prime}
, and

3h-\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}-3C_{0}\sim 2h^{\prime}-C_{0}^{\prime}-\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i},\hskip 10.00002pt4h-2\sum_{i=0}^{r}C_{i}-3C_{0}\sim 3h^{\prime}-2C_{0}^{\prime}-2\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}.

Therefore by (
7.15
)
-K_{X|\widetilde{T}}
is isomorphic to the tautological class for:

(7.16)

{T}\cong\mathbb{P}_{B}\Bigl(\mathcal{O}\bigl(2h^{\prime}-C_{0}^{\prime}-\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})\oplus\mathcal{O}\bigl(3h^{\prime}-2C_{0}^{\prime}-2\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}\bigr)\Bigr).

We have
h^{\prime}\cdot C_{0}^{\prime}=0
and
(h^{\prime})^{2}=6
, thus
h^{\prime}
is nef and big, and Riemann Roch yields
h^{0}(B,h^{\prime})=5
.
Since
h_{0}
is base point free, the linear system
|h^{\prime}|
can have base points only along
C_{0}^{\prime}
. On the other hand
H^{1}(B,h_{0})=0
by Kawamata-Viehweg vanishing, thus the restriction
H^{0}(B,h^{\prime})\to H^{0}(C_{0}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{C_{0}^{\prime}})=\mathbb{C}
is surjective, and no point on
C_{0}^{\prime}
is a base point.

We conclude that
h^{\prime}
is base-point-free; moreover for every irreducible curve
\Gamma\subset B
we have
h^{\prime}\cdot\Gamma=(h_{0}+C_{0}^{\prime})\cdot\Gamma=0
if and only if
\Gamma
is among
C_{0}^{\prime},C_{1},\dotsc,C_{r}
. Finally, since the linear system
|h_{0}|=|h^{\prime}-C_{0}^{\prime}|
defines a birational map,
|h^{\prime}|

defines a birational map
\sigma_{B}^{\prime}\colon B\to\mathbb{P}^{4}
with exceptional locus
C_{0}^{\prime},C_{1},\dotsc,C_{r}
, whose image is a sextic surface
A^{\prime}
with isolated singularities at
q_{0}^{\prime}:=\sigma_{B}^{\prime}(C_{0}^{\prime})
and
q_{i}^{\prime}:=\sigma_{B}^{\prime}(C_{i})
for
i=1,\dotsc,r
.
Thus
\sigma^{\prime}_{B}
factors through
k_{|B}
:

\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k_{|B}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{\prime}_{B}}
\textstyle{{B_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{A^{\prime}}}

where the map
B_{0}\to A^{\prime}
contracts
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}
to
q_{0}^{\prime}
, and its inverse
A^{\prime}\dasharrow B_{0}

is the projection from
q_{0}^{\prime}
. We conclude that
A^{\prime}
does not contain lines through
q_{0}^{\prime}
,
and that
q_{0}^{\prime},\dotsc,q_{r}^{\prime}
are in general linear position in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
, because

p_{1},\dotsc,p_{r}
are in general linear position in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
.
We also note that by generality of
A
,
\overline{p_{i}p_{j}}\not\subset B_{0}
, thus

\overline{q_{i}^{\prime}q_{j}^{\prime}}\not\subset A^{\prime}
for every
i,j
with
1\leq i<j\leq r
.

Let us consider now
\widehat{W}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Bl}_{q_{0}^{\prime},\dotsc,q_{r}^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}^{4}
and its Fano model
W^{\prime}
. Note that since
r\leq 4
, these are toric varieties, and
\widehat{W}^{\prime}\cong\widehat{W}
,
W^{\prime}\cong W
. The transform
S^{\prime}\subset\widehat{W}^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
B
, and it is contained in the open subset where the map
\widehat{W}^{\prime}\dasharrow W^{\prime}
is an isomorphism; we still denote by
S^{\prime}\subset W^{\prime}
its transform.
Then as in the proof of Lemma
7.13
we see that
\mathcal{O}_{B}(2h^{\prime}-C_{0}^{\prime}-\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})
and
\mathcal{O}_{B}(3h^{\prime}-2C_{0}^{\prime}-2\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i})
are both ample on
B
,
and we get the statement by (
7.16
).
∎

Proof of Prop.
7.11
.

If
X
is Fano, then
\rho_{X}\leq 7
by Rem.
7.4
and Th.
1.11
(ii)
.

Conversely, let us assume that
r\leq 4
, and we show that
X
is Fano.
We proceed similarly to the proof of Prop.
7.5
.
Let
T_{i}\subset{W}
be the transform of the cone in
\mathbb{P}^{4}
over
A
with vertex
q_{i}
, for
i=0,\dotsc,r
.

In
{W}
we have

T_{i}\sim 2(2H-\sum_{j=0}^{r}D_{j}-D_{i})
,
\sum_{i=0}^{r}T_{i}\sim 4(r+1)H-2(r+2)\sum_{j=0}^{r}D_{j}
,

-K_{W}=5H-3\sum_{i=0}^{r}D_{i}
,
and

2(r+2)(-K_{W})=2(4-r)H+3\sum_{i=0}^{r}T_{i}
.

Finally let
\widetilde{T}_{i}\subset X
be the transform of
T_{i}
; then
\alpha^{*}(T_{i})=\widetilde{T}_{i}+E
, and we get

(7.17)

2(r+2)(-K_{X})=2(4-r)H_{X}+3\sum_{i=0}^{r}\widetilde{T}_{i}+(r-1)E.

Since
S
is a K3 surface and
K_{S}\sim 0
, by Lemmas
7.13
and
2.4
we have that
-K_{X|E}
is ample.
Moreover in the notation of Lemma
7.14
we have
\widetilde{T}=\widetilde{T}_{0}
and
-K_{X|\widetilde{T}_{0}}
ample. By considering the projection from
q_{i}
instead of
q_{0}
, again by Lemma
7.14
we get that
-K_{X|\widetilde{T}_{i}}
is ample for every
i=0,\dotsc,r
.

Let
r\in\{1,\dotsc,4\}
. Then as in the proof of Prop.
7.5
, using (
7.17
) we show that

-K_{X}
is strictly nef. Moreover

K_{X}^{4}>0
(see Table
7.3
), which gives the statement.
The case
r=0
is simpler and is left to the reader.
∎

\begin{array}[]{||c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c||}\hline\cr\hline\cr r&\rho_{X}&K_{X}^{4}&K_{X}^{2}\cdot c_{2}(X)&h^{2,2}(X)&h^{1,3}(X)&b_{3}(X)&h^{0}(X,-K_{X})&\chi(T_{X})\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr 0&3&180&144&22&1&0&43&-18\\
\hline\cr 1&4&150&132&24&1&0&37&-17\\
\hline\cr 2&5&121&118&27&1&0&31&-16\\
\hline\cr 3&6&93&102&31&1&0&25&-15\\
\hline\cr 4&7&66&84&36&1&0&19&-14\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 7.3.
Numerical invariants of the Fano
4
-folds from §
7.3

7.4.
New families with
\delta_{X}=2
from case
(a)

We keep the notation as in §
7.1
.
Let
A\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
be a general quadric surface containing

q_{1},\dotsc,q_{r}
and not
q_{0}
, in particular
A
is contained in a hyperplane not passing through
q_{0}
;
recall that
A\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Then
S\subset\widehat{W}
is a del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{S}=r+2
, and it is disjoint from the transforms of the lines
\overline{q_{i}q_{j}}
, thus
S
is contained in the open subset where the map
\widehat{W}\dasharrow W
is an isomorphism.
We will show the following.

Proposition 7.18
.

The
4
-fold
X
is Fano if and only if
r\in\{0,1,2\}
. In these cases we have

\rho_{X}=r+3\in\{3,4,5\}
and
\delta_{X}=2
.

Remark 7.19
.

For
r=0
and
\rho_{X}=3
, this Fano
4
-fold is the same as
[
Sec23
,
X^{7}_{1,2}
]
.

The surface
B_{0}\cong A
is a smooth quadric surface through
p_{1},\dotsc,p_{r}
, and
B
is a smooth del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{B}=r+2
and
-K_{Y}=2B
(this is the same as in §
7.2
); moreover

S\cong B
.

We note that
T\subset\widetilde{W}
is the transform of the quadric cone
Q\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
, containing
A
, with vertex
q_{0}
. Then
T
is disjoint from the transforms of the lines
\overline{q_{i}q_{j}}
for
1\leq i<j\leq r
, thus it is contained in the open subset where
the birational map
\widetilde{W}\dasharrow W
is an isomorphism.

Lemma 7.20
.

-K_{X|\widetilde{T}}
is ample.

Proof.

We treat the case
r>0
, the case
r=0
being already in
[
Sec23
]
, see Rem.
7.19
.
We have
-K_{X|\widetilde{T}}\cong-K_{\widetilde{X}|\widetilde{T}}
, thus it is enough to show that
-K_{\widetilde{X}|\widetilde{T}}
is ample.

We follow the same notation for
B
as in the proof of Lemma
7.7
, that is we have
B=\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1},\dotsc,p_{r}}B_{0}\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{r+1\,\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
with exceptional divisors
e_{0},e_{1},\dotsc,e_{r}
over
\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Let
h\in\operatorname{Pic}(B)
be the pullback of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2}}(1)
; then we have

\widetilde{T}\cong T=\mathbb{P}_{B}(\mathcal{E}_{|B})
with
\mathcal{E}_{|B}=\mathcal{O}_{B}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{B}(h-e_{2}-\cdots-e_{r})
.

We can choose the hyperplane
J_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
containing
q_{1},\dotsc,q_{r}
as the hyperplane containing
A
. Then
S\subset\widetilde{W}
is the complete intersection of
J
and
T
, hence

\eta_{|T}\cong\mathcal{O}_{T}(J_{|T})=\mathcal{O}_{T}(S)
.
By construction and adjunction we have

{-K_{\widetilde{X}}}_{|\widetilde{T}}\sim{-K_{\widetilde{W}}}_{|T}-S\cong\pi_{|T}^{*}(-2K_{B}-(h-e_{2}-\cdots-e_{r}))+\eta_{|T}

and
-2K_{B}-(h-e_{2}-\cdots-e_{r})=-K_{B}+2h-e_{0}-e_{1}
,
thus
{-K_{\widetilde{X}}}_{|\widetilde{T}}
is a tautological divisor of
\widetilde{T}\cong\mathbb{P}_{B}(\mathcal{O}(-K_{B}+2h-e_{0}-e_{1})\oplus\mathcal{O}(-2K_{B}))
.
We have that
-K_{B}
and
2h-e_{0}-e_{1}
are respectively an ample and a nef divisor on
B
, thus their sum is ample on
B
, and this concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.21
.

-K_{X|E}
is ample if and only if
r\in\{0,1,2\}
.

Proof.

Again we treat the case
r>0
, the case
r=0
being already in
[
Sec23
]
, see Rem.
7.19
.
We show that
\mathcal{N}_{S/W}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{W}(-K_{W})_{|S}
is ample if and only if
r\in\{0,1,2\}
; this implies the statement by Lemma
2.4
.
Since
S
is contained in the open subset where the map
W\dasharrow\widetilde{W}
is an isomorphism, it is equivalent to work with
\mathcal{N}_{S/{\widetilde{W}}}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{W}}(-K_{\widetilde{W}})_{|S}
.

We keep the same notation as in the proof of Lemma
7.20
. To compute
\mathcal{N}_{S/{\widetilde{W}}}
, we note that
S
is the complete intersection of
J
and
T
, so that

\mathcal{N}_{S/{\widetilde{W}}}=\mathcal{O}_{S}(J_{|S})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{S}(T_{|S})\cong\mathcal{O}_{B}(h-e_{2}-\cdots-e_{r})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{B}(-K_{B}).

By adjunction
{-K_{\widetilde{W}}}_{|S}=-K_{S}+\det\mathcal{N}_{S/{\widetilde{W}}}\cong-2K_{B}+h-e_{2}-\cdots-e_{r}
, so that

\mathcal{N}_{S/{\widetilde{W}}}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{W}}(-K_{\widetilde{W}})_{|S}\cong\mathcal{O}_{B}(-2K_{B})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{B}(-K_{B}+h-e_{2}-\cdots-e_{r}).

Now
-K_{B}
is ample, and both
h
and
h-e_{2}
are nef, thus
\mathcal{N}_{S/{\widetilde{W}}}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{W}}(-K_{\widetilde{W}})_{|S}
is ample
if
r\in\{1,2\}
.
If instead
r\geq 3
, let
\Gamma\subset B
be the transform of the line through the images of
e_{2}
and
e_{3}
in
\mathbb{P}^{2}
; then
\Gamma\sim h-e_{2}-e_{3}
and

(-K_{B}+h-e_{2}-\cdots-e_{r})\cdot\Gamma=0
, thus
\mathcal{N}_{S/{\widetilde{W}}}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{W}}(-K_{\widetilde{W}})_{|S}
is not ample.
∎

Proof of Prop.
7.18
.

If
X
is Fano, then
r\leq 2
by Lemma
7.21
.

For the
converse, we set
r=2
, and show that
X
is Fano.
The proof of the ampleness of
-K_{X}
for
r\leq 1
is similar and easier, and we omit it.

We note that
\widetilde{D}_{0}\subset\widetilde{W}
is the section of
\pi
corresponding to the projection of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{Y}(H_{Y}-G_{1}-G_{2})
onto
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
: then
\widetilde{D}_{0}\cap J=\emptyset
and
-K_{\widetilde{W}}\sim J+\widetilde{D}_{0}+2T
.

Let
\ell_{i,j}\subset\widehat{W}
be the transform of the line
\overline{q_{i}q_{j}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
. The map
\widehat{W}\dasharrow\widetilde{W}
is the flip of
\ell_{0,1}
and
\ell_{0,2}
, while the map
\widetilde{W}\dasharrow W
is the flip (of the transform) of
\ell_{1,2}
, which is an exceptional line contained in
J
and disjoint from
S
. Thus
\widetilde{D}_{0}
is contained in the open subset where the birational map
\widetilde{W}\dasharrow W
is an isomorphism.
We denote by
J^{\prime}\subset W
the transform of
J\subset\widetilde{W}
, so that

J\dasharrow J^{\prime}
is the flop of
\ell_{1,2}
, and

-K_{W}\sim J^{\prime}+D_{0}+2T
.

Lastly,
S\subset W
is the complete intersection of
J^{\prime}
and
T
, and so

(7.21)

-K_{X}\sim\widetilde{J}^{\prime}+D^{\prime}_{0}+2\widetilde{T}+2E,

where
\widetilde{J}^{\prime},D_{0}^{\prime}\subset X
are the transforms of
J^{\prime},D_{0}\subset W
.

We have
-K_{X|\widetilde{T}}
ample by Lemma
7.20
and
-K_{X|E}
ample by Lemma
7.21
, and we show here below that
-K_{X|D_{0}^{\prime}}
and
-K_{X|\widetilde{J}^{\prime}}
are ample as well. Then as in the proof of Prop.
7.5
, using (
7.21
) and that
W
is Fano, we show that

-K_{X}
is strictly nef. Finally, one can check directly that
K_{X}^{4}>0
(see Table
7.4
), which gives the statement.

7.22
.

-K_{X|D_{0}^{\prime}}
is ample.

In fact
D_{0}
is disjoint from
J
, thus from
S
, therefore
-K_{X|D_{0}^{\prime}}\cong-K_{\widetilde{W}|\widetilde{D}_{0}}\cong 3H_{Y}-G_{1}-G_{2}
ample on
Y=\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1},p_{2}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
(this can be checked directly, as
Y
is toric, and
\operatorname{NE}(Y)
is generated by the classes of the lines
C_{G_{i}}\subset G_{i}
for
i=1,2
, and of
\Gamma_{1,2}
transform of the line
\overline{p_{1}p_{2}}
).

7.23
.

-K_{X|\widetilde{J^{\prime}}}
is ample.

We have
\widetilde{J}^{\prime}\cong J^{\prime}\cong Y^{\prime}
where
Y\dasharrow Y^{\prime}
is the flop of
\Gamma_{1,2}
. Again
Y^{\prime}
is toric, and
\operatorname{NE}(Y^{\prime})
is generated by the classes of the flopping curve
\Gamma^{\prime}
and of the transform
F_{i}
of a line through
p_{i}
in
\mathbb{P}^{3}
, for
i=1,2
.

Moreover

-K_{X|\widetilde{J^{\prime}}}\cong-K_{W|J^{\prime}}-S
is the transform of
-K_{\widetilde{W}|J}-S\cong 5H_{Y}-3(G_{1}+G_{2})-B\sim 3H_{Y}-2(G_{1}+G_{2})
. Since
(3H_{Y}-2(G_{1}+G_{2}))\cdot\Gamma_{1,2}=-1
, this gives
-K_{X}\cdot F_{i}=1
for
i=1,2

and
-K_{X}\cdot\Gamma^{\prime}>0
, hence

-K_{X|\widetilde{J^{\prime}}}
is ample.

Lastly, we show that
\delta_{X}=2
. It is not difficult to see that
\operatorname{codim}\mathcal{N}_{1}(\widetilde{J_{0}},X)=2
, thus
\delta_{X}\geq 2
.
Moreover
X
cannot be a product of surfaces, because
W
is not (see for instance
[
Rom19a
, Lemma 2.10]
), thus
\delta_{X}\leq 3
by Th.
1.6
.
If
\delta_{X}=3
, then
\rho_{X}\geq 5
by Th.
1.7
, therefore
\rho_{X}=5
and
r=2
. By classification we see that there are no correspondences between the numerical invariants of
X
and those of Fano
4
-folds with
\delta=3
and
\rho=5
(see Table
7.4
and
[
CR22
, Table 3.4]
). Thus
\delta_{X}=2
and this concludes the proof.
∎

\begin{array}[]{||c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c||}\hline\cr\hline\cr r&\rho_{X}&K_{X}^{4}&K_{X}^{2}\cdot c_{2}(X)&b_{4}(X)=h^{2,2}(X)&b_{3}(X)&h^{0}(X,-K_{X})&\chi(T_{X})\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr 0&3&350&188&4&0&75&7\\
\hline\cr 1&4&303&174&7&0&66&5\\
\hline\cr 2&5&257&158&11&0&57&3\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 7.4.
Numerical invariants of the Fano
4
-folds from §
7.4

7.5.
New families with an elementary rational contraction onto a
3
-fold

Let
Z_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{3}
be a general divisor of type
(1,1)
, and
\pi_{0}\colon Z_{0}\to\mathbb{P}^{3}
the projection. Then
Z_{0}
is a Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Z_{0}}=2
, and there is a point
y_{0}\in\mathbb{P}^{3}
such that
\pi_{0}
is smooth with fiber
\mathbb{P}^{1}
over
\mathbb{P}^{3}\smallsetminus\{y_{0}\}
and
\pi_{0}^{-1}(y_{0})\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, see
[
Kac97
, Ex. 11.1]
.

Let
r\in\{0,\dotsc,7\}
and let
\widetilde{Z}\to Z_{0}
be the blow-up of
r
general fibers of
\pi_{0}
; note that
\pi_{0}
induces an elementary contraction
\pi\colon\widetilde{Z}\to Y:=\operatorname{Bl}_{r\,\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{3}
(in fact
\widetilde{Z}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times Y
), and that there is
y\in Y
such that
\pi
is smooth with fiber
\mathbb{P}^{1}
over
Y\smallsetminus\{y\}
and

\pi^{-1}(y)\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Proposition 7.24
.

For
r\in\{0,\dotsc,4\}
there is a SQM
\widetilde{Z}\dasharrow Z
such that
Z
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{Z}=r+2\in\{2,\dotsc,6\}
, and
Z\dasharrow Y
is an elementary contraction.

Question 7.25
.

Does Prop.
7.24
hold for
r=5,6,7
too?

We note that Prop.
7.24
cannot hold for
r\geq 8
by Th.
1.3
.

Remark 7.26
.

For
r=2
and
\rho_{Z}=4
, the Fano
4
-fold
Z
has the same numerical invariants as
[
FTT24
, Fano 4-7]
(see Table
7.5
). However we do not know whether the two
4
-folds belong to the same family.

Proof of Prop.
7.24
.

Let
X
be the Fano
4
-fold introduced in §
7.2
, with
r\in\{0,\dotsc,4\}
and
\rho_{X}=r+3\in\{3,\dotsc,7\}
; we keep the same notation as in §
7.2
. The divisor
\widetilde{T}\subset X
is
a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle over
B\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{p_{1},\dotsc,p_{r}}B_{0}
, and
B_{0}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Set
r=4
, so that
\rho_{X}=7
. Let us consider
f\colon\widetilde{X}\to Y
and its two factorizations in elementary contractions (see diagrams (
5.7
) and (
7.3
)):

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{W}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{{W^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{W}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{Y}}

By Lemma
5.6
,
\alpha^{\prime}
is an elementary contraction of type
(3,2)
with
\operatorname{Exc}(\alpha^{\prime})=\widetilde{T}
, and
W^{\prime}
is Fano. Recall from p.
7.2
that every fiber of
f
over
Y\smallsetminus\{y\}
is one-dimensional, while
f^{-1}(y)\cong\mathbb{F}_{1}
, and
\tilde{\alpha}_{|f^{-1}(y)}
is a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-bundle. Therefore
\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}(f^{-1}(y))\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
, every non-trivial fiber of
\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}
has dimension one, and the same holds for
\alpha^{\prime}
. By Th.
2.6
,
W^{\prime}
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{W^{\prime}}=6
, and
\alpha^{\prime}

is the blow-up of of a smooth irreducible surface
S^{\prime}\subset W^{\prime}
. We have
S^{\prime}\cong B
, so that
S^{\prime}
is a del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{S^{\prime}}=6
. We also note that
\pi^{\prime}\colon\widetilde{W}^{\prime}\to Y
is smooth with fiber
\mathbb{P}^{1}
over
Y\smallsetminus\{y\}
, and
(\pi^{\prime})^{-1}(y)\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
. We are going to show that
W^{\prime}\cong Z
(for
r=4
).

We have a diagram like (
5.47
) (see also Rem.
7.10
):

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{{X_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{0}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{0}}
\textstyle{{W^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{W}^{\prime}=\widehat{W}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{W^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{W^{\prime}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{0}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{W_{0}=\operatorname{Bl}_{q_{0}}\mathbb{P}^{4}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{4}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{3}}

where
\sigma_{W^{\prime}}
blows-up the
4
smooth fibers
F_{i}:=\pi_{0}^{-1}(p_{i})
,
i=1,\dotsc,4
,
\alpha_{0}^{\prime}
is the blow-up of a surface
S_{0}^{\prime}\cong B_{0}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and
\sigma
blows-up the transforms of
F_{i}
,
i=1,\dotsc,4
(see Lemma
5.46
; we are in case
(a)
, with
E_{1},E_{2}
being
E,\widetilde{T}
).

We note that
W_{0}^{\prime}
is smooth,
\rho_{W_{0}^{\prime}}=2
, and
W_{0}^{\prime}
is Fano by Lemma
5.49
. Moreover
\pi_{0}^{\prime}\colon W_{0}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{3}
is a scroll in the sense of adjunction theory, namely there exists
L\in\operatorname{Pic}(W_{0}^{\prime})
such that
L\cdot F=1
for a general fiber
F
of
\pi_{0}^{\prime}
.
Indeed, let us consider the transform
D_{0}^{\prime}\subset W_{0}^{\prime}
of the exceptional divisor
D_{0}\subset W_{0}
of the blow-up
W_{0}\to\mathbb{P}^{4}
. Since the general fiber of
\pi_{0}
is contained in the open subset where both
\alpha_{0}
and
\alpha_{0}^{\prime}
are isomorphism, we still have
D_{0}^{\prime}\cdot F=1
in
W_{0}^{\prime}
, and we set
L:=\mathcal{O}_{W_{0}^{\prime}}(D_{0}^{\prime})
.

Smooth Fano
4
-folds with
\rho=2
and with a scroll structure over
\mathbb{P}^{3}
have been classified in
[
Lan98
]
, there are 18 possibilities. To identify
W_{0}^{\prime}
, we compute its numerical invariants, and we get
K_{W_{0}^{\prime}}^{4}=432
(see Table
7.5
). A simple computation
of the anticanonical degrees of the 18 Fano
4
-folds from
[
Lan98
]
shows that there is only one case with
K^{4}=432
, and it is precisely
Z_{0}\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{3}
a general divisor of type
(1,1)
(see
[
Lan98
, §9.1.2, case 5]
). We conclude that
\widetilde{Z}=\widetilde{W}^{\prime}
, so it has a SQM to a smooth Fano
4
-fold
Z=W^{\prime}
.
This shows the statement for
r=4
(and
r=0
).

For smaller
r
the statement follows from the case
r=4
. Indeed set
Z=Z_{r}
. For any
r\in\{1,2,3\}
there is a birational map
Z_{4}\dasharrow Z_{r}
given by a SQM followed by the blow-up of
4-r
smooth curves (transforms of fibers of
\pi_{0}
), where
Z_{r}
is a smooth Fano
4
-fold which is a SQM of
\widetilde{Z}_{r}=\widetilde{W}^{\prime}_{r}
.

We conclude that, for any
r\in\{0,\dotsc,4\}
,
\widetilde{T}\subset X
is
the exceptional divisor of the blow-up
\alpha^{\prime}\colon X\to Z
of a smooth irreducible surface
S^{\prime}\subset Z
, where
S^{\prime}
is a del Pezzo surface with
\rho_{S^{\prime}}=r+2
, and
S^{\prime}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
for
r=0
.

We use
\alpha^{\prime}
to
compute the invariants of
Z
from those of
X
(note that for
r=0
we have
Z=Z_{0}=W_{0}^{\prime}
). By the proof of Lemma
7.7
we have that

\mathcal{O}_{X}(-\widetilde{T})_{|\widetilde{T}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}(-\widetilde{T})_{|\widetilde{T}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{T}\bigl(\eta_{T}+\pi_{T}^{*}(K_{B}+M)\bigr)

is the tautological class for

\widetilde{T}=\mathbb{P}_{B}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{B}(-2h+e_{0}+e_{2}+\cdots+e_{r})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{B}(-h+e_{0})\bigr).

We conclude that
\mathcal{N}_{S^{\prime}/Z}\cong\mathcal{O}_{B}(2h-e_{0}-e_{2}-\cdots-e_{r})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{B}(h-e_{0})
, from which we compute
c_{2}(\mathcal{N}_{S^{\prime}/Z})=1
,
(K_{Z|S^{\prime}})^{2}=30-4r
,
K_{S^{\prime}}\cdot K_{Z|S^{\prime}}=16-2r
, and finally using Lemma
2.4
and
[
CCF19
, Lemma 6.25]
the invariants in the table below.
∎

\begin{array}[]{||c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c||}\hline\cr\hline\cr r&\rho_{Z}&K_{Z}^{4}&K_{Z}^{2}\cdot c_{2}(Z)&b_{4}(Z)=h^{2,2}(Z)&b_{3}(Z)&h^{0}(Z,-K_{Z})&\chi(T_{Z})\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr 0&2&432&204&3&0&90&12\\
\hline\cr 1&3&368&188&5&0&78&9\\
\hline\cr 2&4&305&170&8&0&66&6\\
\hline\cr 3&5&243&150&12&0&54&3\\
\hline\cr 4&6&182&128&17&0&42&0\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 7.5.
Numerical invariants of the Fano
4
-folds from §
7.5

7.6.
Other examples

Example 7.27

(Toric Fano
4
-folds)
.

We recall that toric Fano
4
-folds are classified, see
[
Bat99
,
Sat00
]
. In the range
\rho\leq 5
, there are several examples with
\delta=2
, or with a rational contraction onto a
3
-fold. For
\rho=6
the only example with
\delta=2
is
S\times S
,
S=\operatorname{Bl}_{2\text{\it pts}}\mathbb{P}^{2}
.

Example 7.28

(Products with
\mathbb{P}^{1}
)
.

There are 13 Fano
3
-folds
Y
with
\rho_{Y}=4
(see
[
IP99
, §12.5]
and
[
MM03
]
), among which 4 are toric; they have Lefschetz defect
\delta_{Y}=2
(see
[
Cas23
, Lemma 13]
). For each such
Y
,
X=\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y
is a Fano
4
-fold with
\rho_{X}=5
and
\delta_{X}=2
(see Rem.
2.1
).

Example 7.29
.

Some other examples of Fano
4
-folds with
\delta=2
and
\rho\in\{4,5\}
can be obtained as follows.
Consider the classification of
Fano
4
-folds with
\delta=3
(Th.
1.7
,
[
CRS22
, Prop. 1.5]
). Excluding the toric ones, and products, there are four families, with
\rho\in\{5,6\}
. These families are obtained by blowing-up a
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle
Z\to T
, where
T
is
\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, or
\mathbb{F}_{1}
, along three pairwise disjoint smooth irreducible surfaces
S_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
, where
S_{2}
and
S_{3}
are sections of the
\mathbb{P}^{2}
-bundle, and
S_{1}
is a double cover of the base
T
. By blowing-up
Z
only along
S_{1}
and
S_{i}
,
i\in\{2,3\}
, we get a (non-toric) Fano
4
-fold
X
with
\rho_{X}=\rho_{T}+3\in\{4,5\}
and
\delta_{X}=2
.
